    49	/**

    61	export function SectionLaunch({
    62	  title,

    76	  const canRead = showHistory && Boolean(sourceSection) && Boolean(studentId);
    77	
    78	  useEffect(() => {
    79	    if (!canRead) {

    81	      return;
    82	    }

    84	    // the component is gone, must not land. Without the guard a slow first read
    85	    // can overwrite a fast second one and show the wrong section's runs.
    86	    let live = true;
    87	    setHistory({ kind: "loading" });
    88	    readMyRecentSectionGrades(RUNS_SHOWN, {

     7	 *
     8	 * A looping demonstration of one stimulus worked the way the drill asks: each

    19

    37	const DETAILS: { label: string; carried: boolean }[] = [

    47	type Kind = "read" | "tell" | "colour" | "space" | "recall" | "tally" | "hold";
    48	interface Beat {
    49	  kind: Kind;
    50	  i: number;
    51	  ms: number;

    53	
    54	const BEATS: Beat[] = [
    55	  ...[0, 1, 2].flatMap((i): Beat[] => [

    67	  read: "01 · Read it",

    75	
    76	/**
    77	 * Reveals `text` a character at a time across `ms`, starting over each time
    78	 * `on` flips true. When `on` goes false the text stays where it got to, so a
    79	 * retelling remains readable while the colour lands beside it.
    80	 */
    81	function useTypewriter(text: string, on: boolean, ms: number, instant: boolean): string {
    82	  const [n, setN] = useState(instant ? text.length : 0);
    83	  useEffect(() => {
    84	    if (instant) {
    85	      setN(text.length);
    86	      return;
    87	    }
    88	    if (!on) return;
    89	    let raf = 0;
    90	    const t0 = performance.now();
    91	    setN(0);
    92	    const tick = (t: number) => {
    93	      const k = Math.min(1, (t - t0) / ms);
    94	      setN(Math.floor(k * text.length));
    95	      if (k < 1) raf = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    96	    };
    97	    raf = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    98	    return () => cancelAnimationFrame(raf);
    99	  }, [text, on, ms, instant]);
   100	  return text.slice(0, n);
   101	}
   102	
   103	function Waveform({ live }: { live: boolean }) {
   104	  return (
   105	    <span className="rd-wave" data-live={live || undefined} aria-hidden="true">
   106	      {Array.from({ length: 7 }, (_, i) => (
   107	        <i key={i} style={{ animationDelay: `${(i * 0.09).toFixed(2)}s` }} />
   108	      ))}
   109	    </span>
   110	  );
   111	}
   112	
   113	function clock(seconds: number): string {
   114	  const m = Math.floor(seconds / 60);
   115	  const s = seconds % 60;
   116	  return `${m}:${String(s).padStart(2, "0")}`;
   117	}

   328	import type React from "react";

   335	export interface SectionBriefingPageProps {
   336	  title: string;
   337	  subtitle: string | null;
   338	  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
   339	  onStart: () => void;
   340	  onExit?: () => void;
   341	}
   342	
   343	/**
   344	 * The instructions, on their own page, before anything records.
   345	 *
   346	 * Pressing Start on a section used to open the microphone straight away, and
   347	 * the method was folded into tabs on a page she had already scrolled past. This
   348	 * page is the whole briefing laid out in order, every step numbered and open,
   349	 * with one Start button at the top and the same one at the foot. The written
   350	 * instructions sit under it, closed. Nothing here begins the run.
   351	 */
   352	export function SectionBriefingPage({
   353	  title,
   354	  subtitle,
   355	  instructions,
   356	  onStart,
   357	  onExit,
   358	}: SectionBriefingPageProps) {
   359	  const briefing = instructions.briefing;
   360	  const noKeyboard = useNoKeyboard();
   361	  const steps = briefing?.sections.find((s) => (s.steps?.length ?? 0) > 0)?.steps ?? [];
   362	
   363	  const startButton = (label: string) => (
   364	    <button type="button" className="sl-start" onClick={onStart}>
   365	      {label}
   366	      <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />
   367	    </button>
   368	  );
   369	
   370	  return (
   371	    <div className="sl sl-briefing-page">
   372	      <header className="sl-bp-head">
   373	        <div>
   374	          {/* No chip. The title already names the drill, and on the door the
   375	              chip earns its place because the H1 there is the bare name. */}
   376	          <h1 className="sl-title">{title}</h1>
   377	          {(instructions.tagline || subtitle) && (
   378	            <p className="sl-tagline">
   379	              {instructions.tagline ? renderInlineMarks(instructions.tagline, "sl-bp-tag") : subtitle}
   380	            </p>
   381	          )}
   382	        </div>
   383	        <div className="sl-cta">
   384	          {startButton("Start the section")}
   385	          {onExit && (

   387	              Back
   388	            </button>
   389	          )}
   390	        </div>
   391	      </header>
   392	
   393	      {/* One fact, the one that lowers her shoulders before she reads. That the
   394	          section records in one take, and what pausing does and does not buy
   395	          her, is said at the foot, beside
   396	          the Start she presses after reading; saying it here as well made the
   397	          notice a paragraph she skipped. (An earlier version of this comment
   398	          claimed Step 4 carried it. It did not: the sentence lived only in
   399	          `instructions.rules`, which this page never renders, so for a day the
   400	          page did not say it anywhere.) */}
   401	      {/* No role="status". A live region is for state that changes without a page
   402	          load; this sentence is the same every time, and announcing it as an
   403	          update is noise. */}
   404	      <p className="sl-notice">
   405	        Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start.
   406	      </p>
   407	
   408	      {steps.length > 0 && (
   409	        <section className="sl-bp-steps" aria-label="What you do, step by step">
   410	          <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">What you do, step by step</h2>
   411	          <ol className="sl-bp-list">
   412	            {steps.map((step, i) => (
   413	              <li key={i} style={{ "--i": i } as React.CSSProperties}>
   414	                <span className="sl-bp-n">Step {i + 1}</span>
   415	                <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bps-l-${i}`)}</strong>
   416	                <p>{renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bps-b-${i}`)}</p>
   417	              </li>
   418	            ))}
   419	          </ol>
   420	        </section>
   421	      )}
   422	
   423	      {/* The four steps above ARE the instruction. This is the same method told
   424	          again at length, and rendering both open left her reading the method
   425	          twice with no way to tell which one she was meant to follow. Closed by
   426	          default, one click from anyone who wants the long version. */}
   427	      {briefing && (
   428	        <details className="sl-bp-more">
   429	          {/* The summary carries a real heading rather than only looking like one.
   430	              It read as a section title and was absent from the heading outline,
   431	              which is what left the page jumping H2 to H4. */}
   432	          <summary>
   433	            <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">Read the long version</h2>
   434	          </summary>
   435	          <section className="sl-essay sl-bp-essay" aria-label="Full instructions">
   436	          {briefing.lede && <p className="rd-lede">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.lede, "sl-bp-lede")}</p>}
   437	          {briefing.sections.map((section, i) => (
   438	            <section key={i}>
   439	              <h3>{renderInlineMarks(section.heading, `sl-bp-h-${i}`)}</h3>
   440	              {section.body.map((para, j) => (
   441	                <p key={j}>{renderInlineMarks(para, `sl-bp-p-${i}-${j}`)}</p>
   442	              ))}
   443	              {section.steps && (
   444	                <ol>
   445	                  {section.steps.map((step, j) => (
   446	                    <li key={j}>
   447	                      <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bp-sl-${i}-${j}`)}</strong>{" "}
   448	                      {renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bp-sb-${i}-${j}`)}
   449	                    </li>
   450	                  ))}
   451	                </ol>
   452	              )}
   453	              {section.demo && (
   454	                <ol>
   455	                  {section.demo.beats.map((b, j) => (
   456	                    <li key={j}>
   457	                      <strong>{b.kind}</strong> {renderInlineMarks(b.text, `sl-bp-d-${i}-${j}`)}
   458	                    </li>
   459	                  ))}
   460	                </ol>
   461	              )}
   462	            </section>
   463	          ))}
   464	          </section>
   465	        </details>
   466	      )}
   467	
   468	      {/* The two facts she needs at the moment she presses Start, said where
   469	          she presses it: what a pause costs her, and what the time is for. The
   470	          closer is the authored last beat before Start and belongs here too. */}
   471	      <footer className="sl-bp-foot">
   472	        <p className="sl-bp-fact">
   473	          It runs in one take, all the way through, out loud. You can pause: the text
   474	          hides and the clock stops while you are away, and you cannot go back to a
   475	          stimulus. Twenty-five minutes for the whole section is the goal you are
   476	          working toward, not a limit.
   477	        </p>
   478	        {/* The key that runs the drill, said here rather than in the ready beat,
   479	            where it was thirty words to read in three seconds. Here she has as
   480	            long as she likes. */}
   481	        <p className="sl-bp-fact">
   482	          Space twice per stimulus: once when you have read it all, once when you have
   483	          given The Recall.
   484	        </p>
   485	        {/* A phone has no Space bar. The run shows a button in its place, and

   489	        {noKeyboard && (

   491	            On a touch screen there is no Space bar. The run shows a button at the foot of
   492	            the page instead, Next stimulus and then Recall full stimulus, and it does the
   493	            same thing.
   494	          </p>
   495	        )}
   496	        {briefing?.closer && (
   497	          <p className="sl-bp-closer">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.closer, "sl-bp-closer")}</p>

   499	        {/* Says what happens to her, not how the clock works. "Counting up"

34	    34	 * no stimulus at all during The Recall

37	    37	 * **It wears the real section's clothes.** The drill she is training for is sat
38	    38	 * on LawHub, and `OfficialDrillFrame` already mirrors that surface: indigo
39	    39	 * field, white header rail, one centred white paper card, the stimulus in
40	    40	 * Fraunces at 19px. This screen replicates that contract exactly and removes
41	    41	 * the answering half -- no question column, no choices, no verdict, no
42	    42	 * notepad -- so the drill feels like the thing it is preparing her for rather
43	    43	 * than like a separate app. The frame itself is not imported because it carries
44	    44	 * auth, the notepad and the tutor toggle, none of which may exist inside this
45	    45	 * loop, and because this component has to stay presentational.
46	    46	 *
47	    47	 * The hard requirements, all of them negative:
48	    48	 *
49	    49	 * - **No place marker.** The app does not track which chunk she is on, so no
50	    50	 *   chunk is styled as current, done, or upcoming. She reads, recalls and
51	    51	 *   colours in whatever units she likes.
52	    52	 * - **The text truly vanishes for The Recall.** Not blurred, not transparent,
53	    53	 *   not aria-hidden: removed. A stimulus left in the DOM would be read back to
54	    54	 *   a student using a screen reader, which defeats the drill for her exactly.

203	   203	/** mm:ss, and hh:mm:ss only if a section ever ran past an hour. */

298	   298	    <div className="ac-section">

423	   423	             */

491	   491	                      Ask again
492	   492	                    </button>
493	   493	                    {isEmbedded() && (

496	   496	                        className="ac-section-ready-retry"
497	   497	                        onClick={() =>
498	   498	                          window.open(window.location.href, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer")
499	   499	                        }
500	   500	                      >
501	   501	                        Open in its own tab
502	   502	                      </button>
503	   503	                    )}
504	   504	                    {onExit && (
505	   505	                      <button type="button" className="ac-section-ready-leave" onClick={onExit}>
506	   506	                        Leave this section
507	   507	                      </button>
508	   508	                    )}
509	   509	                  </div>
510	   510	                </>

512	   512	              ) : (

562	   562	                      {chunk.text}{" "}

565	   565	                </div>
566	   566	              ) : null}
567	   567	            </div>

608	   608	                </p>

610	   610	              {error ? (

613	   613	                </p>
614	   614	              ) : null}
615	   615	              {/* Not on a device with no Space key: the on-screen button below

618	   618	              <p className="ac-section-hint">
619	   619	                {noKeyboard ? null : <kbd>space</kbd>}{" "}
620	   620	                {paused
621	   621	                  ? "back to it"
622	   622	                  : countdown !== null
623	   623	                    ? micRefused
624	   624	                      ? "once the microphone is on"
625	   625	                      : "in a moment"
626	   626	                    : KEY_DOES[phase]}

634	   634	                <button
635	   635	                  type="button"
636	   636	                  className="ac-section-advance"
637	   637	                  onClick={onAdvance}
638	   638	                  disabled={countdown !== null || paused}
639	   639	                >
640	   640	                  {countdown !== null
641	   641	                    ? micRefused
642	   642	                      ? "Waiting for the microphone"
643	   643	                      : "Starting"
644	   644	                    : KEY_DOES[phase] === "recall full stimulus"
645	   645	                      ? "Recall full stimulus"
646	   646	                      : "Next stimulus"}
647	   647	                </button>
648	   648	              ) : null}
649	   649	            </div>
650	   650	          </div>
651	   651	        </div>
652	   652	
653	   653	        {/* The first-run walkthrough. Beside the paper, never on it, and it

686	   686	import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";
687	   687	import type { SectionPhase } from "./section";
688	   688	
689	   689	/**
690	   690	 * One quiet element that moves with her voice, and the only evidence on screen
691	   691	 * that the microphone is alive.
692	   692	 *
693	   693	 * For twenty-one minutes she talks into a blank card. The recorder already
694	   694	 * reports a REFUSED microphone, but it cannot see a live-but-silent one -- a
695	   695	 * muted input, a device the OS switched underneath her, a headset that dropped
696	   696	 * off. That failure produces a perfect-looking run, a clean upload, and a
697	   697	 * Report Card of zeros, and she finds out at the end of the twenty-one minutes.
698	   698	 *
699	   699	 * So this does two jobs with one reading.
700	   700	 *
701	   701	 * **Presence.** A bar that answers "is anything reaching it" without answering
702	   702	 * "how am I doing". Deliberately not a level meter with a target: nothing here
703	   703	 * may look like a score, because nothing is judged until the section ends.
704	   704	 *
705	   705	 * **The fault.** If the input never leaves the noise floor across a whole
706	   706	 * stimulus, that is said out loud at the boundary, while there is still a
707	   707	 * section left to save -- and unsaid the moment anything is heard again.
708	   708	 *
709	   709	 * Nothing here re-renders the runner. The bar is written to as a DOM node
710	   710	 * inside an animation frame, because a component that set state at 60Hz would
711	   711	 * be re-rendering the drill room underneath her while she reads.
712	   712	 */
713	   713	
714	   714	/**
715	   715	 * The line between "an open microphone in a quiet room" and "nothing arriving".
716	   716	 *
717	   717	 * RMS over the raw time-domain samples, so it is amplitude and not loudness. A
718	   718	 * genuinely dead input sits at or near zero; a live one in a silent room still
719	   719	 * carries the room. Set low on purpose: this exists to catch nothing at all,
720	   720	 * never to judge a quiet speaker, and a false "your microphone is dead" during
721	   721	 * a real run is worse than the fault it reports.
722	   722	 */
723	   723	const NOISE_FLOOR = 0.004;
724	   724	
725	   725	/**
726	   726	 * How long a phase has to run before its silence means anything.
727	   727	 *
728	   728	 * A Recall she ends in three seconds carries no evidence either way, and
729	   729	 * accusing the hardware on that is how a warning stops being believed. A window
730	   730	 * shorter than this counts neither for the fault nor against it.
731	   731	 */
732	   732	const MIN_WINDOW_MS = 8000;
733	   733	
734	   734	/**
735	   735	 * Consecutive silent windows before the fault is raised.
736	   736	 *
737	   737	 * Both halves of a stimulus are spoken -- "Read it. Tell it back and colour it in
738	   738	 * as you go", then "Say the whole argument back from memory" -- so a dead input
739	   739	 * is silent through every window there is. A skip is silent through exactly
740	   740	 * one: she had nothing for that Recall, and then she reads the next stimulus
741	   741	 * aloud like all the others.
742	   742	 *
743	   743	 * One window was therefore the wrong unit, and it accused the hardware on the
744	   744	 * most ordinary thing a student does. Two consecutive is the shortest run a skip
745	   745	 * cannot produce, and it costs a genuinely dead microphone one further stimulus
746	   746	 * before it is named.
747	   747	 */
748	   748	const SILENT_WINDOWS = 2;
749	   749	
750	   750	/** Full deflection. Chosen so ordinary speech uses most of the bar. */
751	   751	const FULL_SCALE = 0.15;
752	   752	
753	   753	export interface VoiceLevelProps {
754	   754	  /** The live input, or null when the microphone never opened. */
755	   755	  getStream: () => MediaStream | null;
756	   756	  phase: SectionPhase;
757	   757	  /**
758	   758	   * Whether nothing is reaching the microphone, called only when the answer
759	   759	   * changes.
760	   760	   *
761	   761	   * It used to be a one-way announcement, fired once and never taken back, and
762	   762	   * the runner had no setter that could clear it. One deliberate skip therefore
763	   763	   * told a student her hardware was broken, and went on telling her for the
764	   764	   * remaining twenty-five stimuli and four minutes of clearly heard speech.
765	   765	   */
766	   766	  onDeadInput?: (dead: boolean) => void;
767	   767	  /**
768	   768	   * The first time anything at all reaches the microphone. Called once.
769	   769	   *
770	   770	   * The ready beat holds on this. "Say something to check your microphone"
771	   771	   * was a three-second wish: the beat counted to zero whether or not the bar
772	   772	   * had ever moved, so the sentence was an instruction nothing waited for. The
773	   773	   * beat now waits for this, or for its own cap, whichever is first.
774	   774	   */
775	   775	  onHeard?: () => void;
776	   776	}
777	   777	
778	   778	export function VoiceLevel({ getStream, phase, onDeadInput, onHeard }: VoiceLevelProps) {
779	   779	  const bar = useRef<HTMLSpanElement>(null);
780	   780	  const level = useRef(0);
781	   781	  /** The loudest thing heard since the current phase began. */
782	   782	  const peak = useRef(0);
783	   783	  const windowStartedAt = useRef<number | null>(null);
784	   784	  /** Consecutive windows that ran long enough to count and carried nothing. */
785	   785	  const silentWindows = useRef(0);
786	   786	  /** What the runner has been told, so it is told only when that changes. */
787	   787	  const dead = useRef(false);
788	   788	  const onDead = useRef(onDeadInput);
789	   789	  onDead.current = onDeadInput;
790	   790	  /** Whether the first sound has been reported, so it is reported once. */
791	   791	  const heard = useRef(false);
792	   792	  const onFirstSound = useRef(onHeard);
793	   793	  onFirstSound.current = onHeard;
794	   794	
795	   795	  /*
796	   796	   * One analyser for the life of the run.
797	   797	   *
798	   798	   * Opened once and closed once, like the recorder itself: an AudioContext per
799	   799	   * phase would be forty-four of them across a section, and Chrome caps how
800	   800	   * many a page may hold at the same time.
801	   801	   */
802	   802	  useEffect(() => {
803	   803	    let raf = 0;
804	   804	    let context: AudioContext | null = null;
805	   805	    let stopped = false;
806	   806	
807	   807	    const attach = () => {
808	   808	      if (stopped) return;
809	   809	      const stream = getStream();
810	   810	      // The recorder opens the microphone asynchronously and this mounts with
811	   811	      // it, so the stream is usually a moment away rather than absent. Ask
812	   812	      // again next frame; a genuinely refused microphone simply never arrives
813	   813	      // and the bar stays flat, which is the truth.
814	   814	      if (!stream) {
815	   815	        raf = requestAnimationFrame(attach);
816	   816	        return;
817	   817	      }
818	   818	      const Ctor =
819	   819	        window.AudioContext ??
820	   820	        (window as unknown as { webkitAudioContext?: typeof AudioContext }).webkitAudioContext;
821	   821	      if (!Ctor) return;
822	   822	      context = new Ctor();
823	   823	      const analyser = context.createAnalyser();
824	   824	      // Small window, because this is an amplitude reading and not a spectrum:
825	   825	      // it costs less per frame and follows her voice more closely.
826	   826	      analyser.fftSize = 512;
827	   827	      context.createMediaStreamSource(stream).connect(analyser);
828	   828	      const samples = new Float32Array(analyser.fftSize);
829	   829	
830	   830	      const read = () => {
831	   831	        analyser.getFloatTimeDomainData(samples);
832	   832	        let sum = 0;
833	   833	        for (let i = 0; i < samples.length; i++) sum += samples[i] * samples[i];
834	   834	        const rms = Math.sqrt(sum / samples.length);
835	   835	        if (rms > peak.current) peak.current = rms;
836	   836	        /*
837	   837	         * Recovery is immediate, and deliberately not deferred to the next
838	   838	         * boundary like the fault is. The fault needs a whole window to be sure;
839	   839	         * being heard again needs one frame, and a warning that outlives the
840	   840	         * condition it describes is the thing being fixed. Guarded on `dead` so
841	   841	         * this is a transition rather than a call at 60Hz.
842	   842	         */
843	   843	        if (rms >= NOISE_FLOOR && !heard.current) {
844	   844	          heard.current = true;
845	   845	          onFirstSound.current?.();
846	   846	        }
847	   847	        if (rms >= NOISE_FLOOR && dead.current) {
848	   848	          dead.current = false;
849	   849	          silentWindows.current = 0;
850	   850	          onDead.current?.(false);
851	   851	        }
852	   852	        // Rises fast and falls slowly, so the bar tracks speech rather than
853	   853	        // flickering on every syllable boundary.
854	   854	        level.current = rms > level.current ? rms : level.current * 0.88 + rms * 0.12;
855	   855	        if (bar.current) {
856	   856	          bar.current.style.transform = `scaleX(${Math.min(1, level.current / FULL_SCALE)})`;
857	   857	        }
858	   858	        raf = requestAnimationFrame(read);
859	   859	      };
860	   860	      read();
861	   861	    };
862	   862	
863	   863	    attach();
864	   864	    return () => {
865	   865	      stopped = true;
866	   866	      cancelAnimationFrame(raf);
867	   867	      void context?.close();
868	   868	    };
869	   869	  }, [getStream]);
870	   870	
871	   871	  /*
872	   872	   * The fault, decided at the boundary rather than during a phase.
873	   873	   *
874	   874	   * Judging it while she is mid-sentence would fire on a long pause. Judging it
875	   875	   * as a phase closes uses the whole window, which is the only reading that
876	   876	   * separates "she went quiet" from "nothing is arriving".
877	   877	   *
878	   878	   * Every boundary is judged now, not only the end of a Recall. The working
879	   879	   * phase is spoken too, so its silence is evidence of exactly the same kind,
880	   880	   * and using both is what lets a run of them mean something a single one
881	   881	   * cannot.
882	   882	   */
883	   883	  useEffect(() => {
884	   884	    const started = windowStartedAt.current;
885	   885	    const loudest = peak.current;
886	   886	    peak.current = 0;

    10	
    11	/**
    12	 * The wait between finishing a section and seeing the Report Card.
    13	 *
    14	 * Grading is a post-section job: one transcription of ~25 minutes of audio plus
    15	 * one judgement call per stimulus, run server-side after she stops. It does not
    16	 * finish inside the request that ends the section, and it must not be tied to
    17	 * this tab staying open.
    18	 *
    19	 * So this polls for the grade rather than waiting on a response. The ABSENCE of
    20	 * a row is the pending signal (ADR 0019): there is no status column to trust,
    21	 * and none she could set herself. If it never arrives, the honest thing to show
    22	 * is that it is still being marked, not an error and certainly not a zero. The
    23	 * sweeper picks up anything this tab missed.
    24	 */
    25	
    26	const POLL_MS = 4000;
    27	/** After this, stop asking and tell her it is still being marked. */
    28	const GIVE_UP_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000;
    29	/**
    30	 * How long to wait before asking for the grade a second time.
    31	 *
    32	 * The runner already asked once, fire and forget, and that ask can be lost: the
    33	 * tab closed before it left, the network dropped, the function was cold. One
    34	 * repeat after a decent wait recovers all three.
    35	 *
    36	 * The wait has to be longer than a grading actually takes. At 20 seconds the
    37	 * repeat arrived while the first grading was still transcribing, nothing was on
    38	 * record yet, and both ran to the end: two transcriptions, two sets of verdicts
    39	 * on the same audio, and a card that showed the first while the database kept
    40	 * the second. A real section grades in one to three minutes. The grader now
    41	 * also refuses to start while another grading of the same Attempt is running,
    42	 * so a repeat that does land early costs one request rather than a second
    43	 * grading; this wait is what keeps that from being the ordinary path.
    44	 */
    45	const RETRY_ASK_MS = 120000;
    46	
    47	interface SectionReportProps {
    48	  assignmentId: string;
    49	  onDone?: () => void;
    50	}
    51	
    52	export function SectionReport({ assignmentId, onDone }: SectionReportProps) {
    53	  const navigate = useNavigate();
    54	  const [grade, setGrade] = useState<SectionGrade | null>(null);
    55	  const [stillWaiting, setStillWaiting] = useState(false);
    56	  /**
    57	   * Bumped by "Check again", which restarts the effect and so starts a fresh
    58	   * give-up window. Before this, giving up was terminal: the screen stopped
    59	   * asking for ever and the only way to find out was to leave and come back,
    60	   * which is a thing she had to guess at.
    61	   */
    62	  const [round, setRound] = useState(0);
    63	  const startedAt = useRef(Date.now());
    64	  /** Minutes since she finished, for a wait that says how long it has been. */
    65	  const [waitedMs, setWaitedMs] = useState(0);
    66	
    67	  useEffect(() => {
    68	    if (grade) return;
    69	    const tick = setInterval(() => setWaitedMs(Date.now() - startedAt.current), 10000);
    70	    return () => clearInterval(tick);
    71	  }, [grade]);
    72	
    73	  useEffect(() => {
    74	    let cancelled = false;
    75	    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>;
    76	    let askedAgain = false;
    77	    /*
    78	     * Measured per round, not from the first paint, so a "Check again" gets a
    79	     * full window rather than giving up on its first tick.
    80	     */
    81	    const roundStartedAt = Date.now();
    82	
    83	    const poll = async () => {
    84	      try {
    85	        const found = await getSectionGrade(assignmentId);
    86	        if (cancelled) return;
    87	        if (found) {
    88	          setGrade(found);
    89	          return;
    90	        }
    91	      } catch {
    92	        // A failed read is not a failed run. Keep waiting; the row either
    93	        // arrives or the give-up copy takes over.
    94	      }
    95	      if (cancelled) return;
    96	
    97	      const waited = Date.now() - roundStartedAt;
    98	      if (!askedAgain && waited > RETRY_ASK_MS) {
    99	        // Ask once more. Losing the first ask is the likeliest way a finished
   100	        // run ends up with no Report Card, and it is the one failure this
   101	        // screen is in a position to repair by itself.
   102	        askedAgain = true;
   103	        void requestSectionGrading(assignmentId);
   104	      }
   105	      if (waited > GIVE_UP_MS) {
   106	        setStillWaiting(true);
   107	        return;
   108	      }
   109	      timer = setTimeout(poll, POLL_MS);
   110	    };
   111	
   112	    void poll();
   113	    return () => {
   114	      cancelled = true;
   115	      clearTimeout(timer);
   116	    };
   117	  }, [assignmentId, round]);
   118	
   119	  if (grade)
   120	    return (
   121	      <SectionReportCard
   122	        grade={grade}
   123	        onDone={onDone}
   124	        onStartFocusSet={async (slugs) => {
   125	          // A fresh Attempt built server-side from this run's weakest stimuli,
   126	          // entered straight into the runner the way the library enters it.
   127	          const id = await startSectionFocusAttempt(assignmentId, slugs);
   128	          navigate(`/activities/${id}`, { state: { startSection: true } });
   129	        }}
   130	      />
   131	    );
   132	
   133	  /** Whole minutes, for a sentence rather than a counter she watches. */
   134	  const minutesWaited = Math.floor(waitedMs / 60000);
   135	
   136	  /*
   137	   * The wait is the card's first page, not the shell's last one.
   138	   *
   139	   * It used to be an Activity Centre panel: the Centre's cream surface, the
   140	   * Centre's border and its muted ink, sitting on the violet ground the report
   141	   * phase paints for the Report Card. So the ten seconds after twenty-five
   142	   * unbroken minutes crossed three grounds -- the runner's indigo, a warm
   143	   * cream panel, then the card -- and the last thing she saw before being told
   144	   * how it went was a colour that belonged to neither surface. Inside `.rc` it
   145	   * is the same violet-cast white the card is, on the same ground, and the
   146	   * card replacing it is a change of content rather than a change of place.
   147	   *
   148	   * `.rc` is what paints `--rc-page`, so the rounding and the border sit on
   149	   * that same element: a square page-coloured box behind a rounded border
   150	   * would show its corners over the violet ground.
   151	   */
   152	  return (
   153	    <div className="rc mx-auto max-w-2xl overflow-hidden rounded-[28px] border border-[var(--rc-hairline)] shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)]">
   154	      <div className="p-8 text-center md:p-12">
   155	        <p className="rc__label">Section finished</p>
   156	        {/*
   157	          * One live region over both states.
   158	          *
   159	          * The heading and the sentence both change without a page load, and
   160	          * neither was announced: a screen reader user was left on "Marking
   161	          * your run" with no way to know it had become "Still being marked",
   162	          * or that the card had arrived. `aria-live` sits on the wrapper so the
   163	          * pair is read as one update rather than two.
   164	          */}
   165	        <div role="status" aria-live="polite">
   166	          <p className="rc__display mt-3 text-[34px] text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
   167	            {stillWaiting ? "Still being marked" : "Marking your run"}
   168	          </p>
   169	          <p className="mt-4 text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
   170	            {stillWaiting
   171	              ? `This one has been marking for ${minutesWaited} minutes, which is longer than it should take. The marking carries on whether or not you stay on this page, and your Report Card will be on this section in the Activity Centre when it lands.`
   172	              : "Your recording is being read back against every detail. That usually takes one to three minutes."}
   173	          </p>
   174	        </div>
   175	        {/*
   176	          * Past the give-up, two real actions.

   178	          * There used to be one button, "Done", and giving up was terminal:
   179	          * the screen stopped asking, said her card would be there "when you
   180	          * come back to it", and gave her nothing to come back with. "Check
   181	          * again" restarts the polling and the ask; the other button says

   183	          */}
   184	        <div className="mt-8 flex flex-wrap items-center justify-center gap-3">
   185	          {stillWaiting && (

.sl-beats {

.sl-beat {
  appearance: none;
  cursor: pointer;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  text-align: left;
  font: inherit;
  color: inherit;
  padding: 0 0 26px;

  grade,
  onDone,

  loadModelRuns = listModelRuns,
}: SectionReportCardProps) {
  const { section, stimuli } = grade;
  /*
   * One open at a time, and the first one open on arrival.
   *
   * The card used to open with twenty-two closed rows, which meant the default
   * state of the screen she waited twenty-five minutes for was a list of
   * headings. Something is always open now, and the ledger says which.
   */
  const [openStimulus, setOpenStimulus] = useState<number>(0);
  /** The ledger's buttons, so the arrow keys can move focus with the selection. */
  const tickRefs = useRef<(HTMLButtonElement | null)[]>([]);
  const clip = useClip();
  const modelRuns = useModelRuns(grade.source_section, loadModelRuns);
  /** Her own recording, when the upload survived. */
  const hers =
    section.audio_bucket && section.audio_path
      ? { bucket: section.audio_bucket, path: section.audio_path }
      : null;

  const overall = useMemo(() => {
    const perfect = stimuli.filter((s) => s.percent === 100).length;
    const atStandard = stimuli.filter((s) => s.band === "at-the-standard").length;
    /*
     * The run's own band. Computed from the mean the same way a stimulus's is
     * from its own percent, so the headline and the ledger cannot disagree
     * about what 86 means.
     */
    const band = BAND[bandFor(section.mean_coverage)];
    /*
     * Whether the marking of this run can be trusted, in her terms.
     *
     * Two measurements, neither of which is about her. `decoy_failures` counts
     * stimuli where the judge credited a Detail belonging to a different
     * stimulus, which means it credited words she never said. `ungraded_count`
     * counts stimuli the judge never answered for, which are otherwise counted
     * as missed -- scoring her for our outage.
     */
    const suspect = (section.decoy_failures ?? 0) > 0;
    const unmarked = section.ungraded_count ?? 0;
    /** Whether The Read was measured at all. Older grades predate it. */
    const readMeasured = section.read_given_count !== undefined;
    /**
     * New grades carry both the assignment's size and each row's authored
     * position. Older grades carry neither, so they keep their historical
     * array-order rendering rather than having a section shape invented for
     * them from a slug.
     */
    const hasStoredPositions = stimuli.every((s) => s.item_order !== undefined);
    const sectionSize = hasStoredPositions ? section.stimuli_in_section : undefined;
    const missingRows = sectionSize === undefined ? 0 : Math.max(0, sectionSize - stimuli.length);

    /*
     * How she ran the section, as against how she did on it.
     *
     * Every one of these was measured by the grader and some were shown
     * nowhere. They are collected rather than rendered inline so a run with
     * nothing to say about it shows nothing at all, instead of an empty heading.
     */
    const notes: string[] = [];
    if (section.reciting_count > 0)
      notes.push(`${section.reciting_count} said back close to word for word`);
    if (section.listing_count > 0)
      notes.push(`${section.listing_count} told as a list rather than an argument`);
    if (section.uncoloured_count > 0)
      notes.push(`${section.uncoloured_count} with no reaction of your own`);
    if (section.worst_dead_air_seconds >= section.dead_air_threshold_seconds)
      notes.push(`Longest freeze inside a recall: ${section.worst_dead_air_seconds}s`);
    if ((section.chunks_read_aloud ?? 0) > 0)
      notes.push(
        `You read ${section.chunks_read_aloud} of them out loud before recalling. That is not marked against you, and reading silently is the harder, better version.`,
      );

    return {
      perfect,
      atStandard,
      band,
      next: nextStep(section, stimuli),
      suspect,
      unmarked,
      readMeasured,
      notes,
      sectionSize,
      missingRows,
    };
  }, [stimuli, section]);

  const s = stimuli[Math.min(openStimulus, stimuli.length - 1)];
  const key = `s${openStimulus}`;
  const band = s ? (s.reciting ? RECITED : BAND[s.band]) : null;
  const clipWindow = s?.recall_window_ms ?? s?.window_ms;
  const model = s ? modelRuns[s.slug] : undefined;
  const pairs = model?.walkthrough ? walkthroughPairs(model.walkthrough) : [];
  // Paired only when the standard's run has exactly one recall per authored
  // line; anything else is shown whole, as before.
  const pairedByLine = !!s?.lines?.length && pairs.length === s.lines.length;
  const held = s ? whatHeld(s) : [];
  /*
   * The highlight follows her own voice only.
   *
   * The grader stores a start and an end for every line SHE spoke. It stores
   * nothing of the kind for the standard's recording, so a card that
   * highlighted through both would be inventing the timings for one of them.
   */
  const hersPlaying =
    clip.playing === key || (clip.playing?.startsWith(`${key}-l`) ?? false);
  const activeLine = lineAtPlayhead(s?.lines, clip.positionMs, hersPlaying);

  return (
    <div className="rc mx-auto max-w-[820px] overflow-hidden rounded-[20px] border border-[var(--rc-hairline)] shadow-[0_1px_2px_rgba(20,20,30,0.04),0_12px_32px_-12px_rgba(20,20,30,0.12)]">

        {overall.next && (

          </p>

        {/*

          * Coverage answers "did you hold it". The Read answers "did you

            note={

              note={

                  ? `where you found the move. On ${section.no_read_count} you did not give a Read at all.`
                  : "where you found the move the argument turns on"
              }
            />
          )}
          <div>
            <p className="rc__label rc__label--onconsole">Time</p>
            <p className="rc__display mt-1.5 text-[28px] text-[var(--rc-console-ink)]">
              {minutes(section.total_seconds)}
            </p>
            <p className="mt-1 text-xs leading-snug text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">
              against a {section.goal_minutes} minute goal
            </p>
            {/*
              * The breaks, said out loud.
              *
              * The figure above is her drill time and correctly has the pauses

            {(section.paused_seconds ?? 0) > 0 && (
              <p className="mt-1.5 text-xs leading-snug text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">
                {minutes(section.paused_seconds ?? 0)} paused across{" "}
                {section.pause_count === 1 ? "one break" : `${section.pause_count} breaks`}, which
                the time above does not include.
              </p>
            )}
            {/*
              * The goal counts only if every stimulus was recalled, coloured and

              *
              * Inside-the-clock is DERIVED from the two numbers above rather
              * than read off `within_time`, which is optional: a run graded

              */}
            {insideTime(section) && !section.within_goal && (
              <p className="mt-1.5 text-xs leading-snug text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">

            {!insideTime(section) && (

## user

Tool result:
 * One control, one row, no workflow. It is under the transcript rather than at
 * the card's head because it is about ONE stimulus's verdict, and because the
 * clip and the transcript are right there -- she can check before she claims,
 * which is what makes the claim worth storing.
 *
 * It is drawn as a quiet text button, not a call to action. The card must not
 * invite a student to dispute every band she dislikes; it must make it possible
 * to report the specific failure the audit found, where the app misheard her
 * and marked her down for its own mistake.
 *
 * **It never claims success it did not have.** The table arrives with its own
 * migration, so on a database that has not had it applied the insert fails, and
 * a control that showed "Noted" anyway would be lying on the one screen whose
 * subject is the app getting her wrong. `not-configured` says so plainly.
 */
function DisputeControl({
  grade,
  stimulus,
}: {
  grade: SectionGrade;
  stimulus: SectionStimulusGrade;
}) {
  type State = "idle" | "sending" | "noted" | "unavailable" | "failed";
  const [state, setState] = useState<State>("idle");

  const send = useCallback(async () => {
    setState("sending");
    const result = await raiseDispute({
      assignmentId: grade.assignment_id,
      attemptNo: grade.attempt_no,
      stimulusSlug: stimulus.slug,
      sourceSection: grade.source_section,
      studentId: grade.student_id,
      band: stimulus.reciting ? "recited" : stimulus.band,
      reason: stimulus.readReason || null,
    });
    if (result.ok) {
      setState("noted");
      return;
    }
    // Narrowed by hand rather than by the discriminant: this project runs with
    // `strictNullChecks` off, which weakens union narrowing here.
    const failure = result as { ok: false; reason: "not-configured" | "failed" };
    setState(failure.reason === "not-configured" ? "unavailable" : "failed");
  }, [grade, stimulus]);

  const settled = state === "noted" || state === "unavailable" || state === "failed";
  const said =
    state === "noted"
      ? "Noted. Joshua will see this beside your Report Card."
      : state === "unavailable" || state === "failed"
        ? "That did not save. Tell Joshua directly and he will look at the recording."
        : "";

  /*
   * One status region, mounted from the start, and the button beside it until
   * it has done its job. The three states used to be three different elements
   * swapped in and out, so the region a reader was told to watch was never the
   * one that changed.
   */
  return (
    <>
      {!settled && (
        <button
          type="button"
          className="mt-2 text-xs text-[var(--rc-ink-faint)] underline underline-offset-2 hover:text-[var(--rc-ink)] disabled:opacity-60"
          onClick={send}
          disabled={state === "sending"}
        >
          {state === "sending" ? "Saving..." : "This is not what I said"}
        </button>
      )}
      <p className="mt-2 text-xs text-[var(--rc-ink-faint)]" role="status">
        {said}
      </p>
    </>
  );
}

/**
 * "Run these again."
 *
 * The one thing on the card that asks for work. It appears only when the run
 * left something weak behind -- a band below the standard, a recital, a
 * stimulus with no reaction of her own, or a Read that was not the move the key
 * names. A run with none of those shows nothing here at all, because being made
 * to redo work you did properly is the fastest way to teach someone that the
 * Report Card is not worth reading.
 *
 * Pressing it builds a fresh Attempt containing only those stimuli, run exactly
 * the way the full section runs, recorded, saved and graded the same way. Its
 * own Report Card is the check on whether the quality bar has been cleared.
 */
function FocusSetPanel({
  grade,
  onStart,
}: {
  grade: SectionGrade;
  onStart: (slugs: string[]) => Promise<void> | void;
}) {
  const set = useMemo(() => pickFocusSet(grade), [grade]);
  const [starting, setStarting] = useState(false);
  const [failed, setFailed] = useState(false);

  if (!set) {
    return (
      <div className="border-t border-[var(--rc-hairline)] px-6 py-6 md:px-9">
        <h2 className="rc__label">Nothing to run again</h2>
        <p className="mt-2 max-w-[58ch] text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
          Every stimulus in this run came back at the standard, said in your own words with a
          reaction of your own. There is no focus set for this one.
        </p>
      </div>
    );
  }

  const go = async () => {
    setFailed(false);
    setStarting(true);
    try {
      await onStart(set.slugs);
    } catch {
      setFailed(true);
      setStarting(false);
    }
  };

  return (
    <div className="border-t border-[var(--rc-hairline)] px-6 py-7 md:px-9">
      <h2 className="rc__label">Your focus set</h2>
      <p className="mt-2 max-w-[58ch] text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
        {set.weakCount === 1
          ? "One stimulus fell short of the standard."
          : `${set.weakCount} stimuli fell short of the standard.`}{" "}
        Run these {set.slugs.length} again on their own, one recording, marked the same way.
      </p>

      <ul className="mt-4 flex flex-wrap gap-2">
        {/* `item_order` is the AUTHORED position and is 1-based, which is how
            the ledger, the open stimulus and its heading all read it. Here it
            was `(item_order ?? i) + 1`, so every chip in the focus set named
            the stimulus after the one it meant: she was sent to run stimulus 5
            again over a fault in stimulus 4. The fallback keeps its `+ 1`,
            because `i` is an array index and 0-based. */}
        {set.stimuli.map((s, i) => (
          <li key={s.slug} className="rc__chip rc__chip--dropped">
            Stimulus {s.item_order ?? i + 1} · {s.reciting ? "said back word for word" : `${s.percent}%`}
          </li>
        ))}
      </ul>

      <div className="mt-5 flex items-center gap-4">
        <button type="button" className="rc__cta" onClick={() => void go()} disabled={starting}>
          {starting ? "Setting it up..." : `Run these ${set.slugs.length} again`}
        </button>
        <span className="text-xs text-[var(--rc-ink-faint)]">
          Optional. This section and its recording are saved either way.
        </span>
      </div>

      {/* Always mounted, so the change is what gets announced. A region that
          arrives with its text already inside it is silent to most readers. */}
      <p className="mt-3 text-xs text-[var(--rc-ink-faint)]" role="status">
        {failed ? "That did not start. Nothing has been lost. Try again in a moment." : ""}
      </p>
    </div>
  );
}

.rc {
  /* ── The console: a deep plum field ─────────────────────────────────── */
  --rc-console: oklch(0.245 0.058 312);
  --rc-console-raised: oklch(0.315 0.062 312);
  --rc-console-hairline: oklch(0.385 0.055 312);
  --rc-console-ink: oklch(0.968 0.014 312);
  --rc-console-ink-dim: oklch(0.815 0.035 312);
  --rc-console-ink-faint: oklch(0.7 0.05 312);

  /* ── The page: a violet-cast white, never cream, never grey ─────────── */
  --rc-page: oklch(0.979 0.008 312);
  --rc-card: oklch(0.995 0.004 312);
  --rc-fill: oklch(0.958 0.012 312);
  --rc-ink: oklch(0.235 0.032 312);
  --rc-ink-muted: oklch(0.475 0.03 312);
  --rc-ink-faint: oklch(0.505 0.035 312);
  --rc-hairline: oklch(0.9 0.014 312);
  --rc-hairline-soft: oklch(0.945 0.011 312);

  /* ── The four categories. Each is a KIND of feedback, not a severity. ─
     `-ink` is the text weight, measured to clear 4.5:1 on both the page and
     its own soft field; `-soft` is that field; `-lit` is the console version,
     which has to clear the same bar against a dark plum instead. */
  --rc-detail-ink: oklch(0.475 0.135 12);
  --rc-detail-soft: oklch(0.952 0.028 12);
  --rc-detail-lit: oklch(0.775 0.115 14);

  --rc-read-ink: oklch(0.46 0.095 218);
  --rc-read-soft: oklch(0.948 0.03 218);
  --rc-read-lit: oklch(0.79 0.1 210);

  --rc-colour-ink: oklch(0.475 0.105 72);
  --rc-colour-soft: oklch(0.955 0.04 82);
  --rc-colour-lit: oklch(0.83 0.13 82);

  --rc-method-ink: oklch(0.455 0.09 278);
  --rc-method-soft: oklch(0.951 0.026 278);
  --rc-method-lit: oklch(0.79 0.085 280);

  /* Sound. Not a category, and the only thing on the page that moves. Held at
     328 rather than nearer the reds: the playhead lands on the same line as the
     dropped-Detail chips, and twenty degrees of hue is not enough separation
     between "this is playing" and "this is what you lost". */
  --rc-signal: oklch(0.545 0.225 328);
  --rc-signal-bright: oklch(0.715 0.2 328);
  --rc-signal-soft: oklch(0.957 0.032 328);
  --rc-signal-ink: oklch(0.995 0.005 328);

  /* The two faint inks are set where they are because that is where they clear
     4.5:1 on their own ground, measured in the browser rather than assumed.
     Every label on this card is 10px, so there is no large-text exemption to
     lean on. */
  --rc-display: "Fraunces", "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;
  --rc-body: "Lexend", "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;
  --rc-mono: "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace;

  /* Exponential ease. No bounce anywhere on this page: it is a page about
     being told what you got wrong, and a spring reads as a toy. */
  --rc-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);

  background: var(--rc-page);
  color: var(--rc-ink);
  font-family: var(--rc-body);
}

/* The card is read on a laptop in a long sitting, so the body column is capped
   at a measure rather than at the shell's container width. */
.rc__measure {
  margin-inline: auto;
  max-width: 72ch;
}
/* The measure held the card's column and not the prose inside the open
   stimulus, whose paragraphs ran the full 625px at 14px: 83 to 104 characters
   a line on the words she reads most closely. The cap belongs on the prose. */
.rc__movement p,
.rc__movement li,
.rc__movement blockquote {
  /* 60ch, not 72: Lexend's zero is wide (8.5px at 14px), so 72ch is 612px and
     about 80 real characters. 60ch measures 510px, 66 characters. */
  max-width: 60ch;
}

/* ── Micro-labels ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   One label style for the whole card. Every section of a stimulus wears it,
   which is what makes the order legible: she learns four labels once and then
   knows where she is on all twenty-two. */
.rc__label {
  color: var(--rc-ink-faint);
  font-family: var(--rc-mono);
  font-size: 10px;
  font-weight: 500;
  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
}

.rc__label--onconsole {
  color: var(--rc-console-ink-faint);
}

/* The card's two headings.
 *
 * Each wraps a small label and a display line so the heading reads as one
 * string ("Report Card, Attempt 2, At the standard") while keeping the two type
 * sizes it had when both were paragraphs. Tailwind's preflight already flattens
 * heading size and weight; this keeps the margin off and lets the children own
 * their own type.
 */
.rc__title {
  margin: 0;
  font: inherit;
  letter-spacing: normal;
}

.rc__title > .rc__label {
  display: block;
}

.rc__display {
  font-family: var(--rc-display);
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 96;

            * through a row of marks. A toolbar with a roving tabindex is the
            * pattern for a group like this: Tab lands on whichever stimulus is

            */}
          <div
            className="rc__ledger mt-3.5"
            role="toolbar"
            aria-label="Stimulus by stimulus"
            aria-orientation="horizontal"
            onKeyDown={(e) => {
              const last = stimuli.length - 1;
              const to =
                e.key === "ArrowRight" || e.key === "ArrowDown"
                  ? Math.min(openStimulus + 1, last)
                  : e.key === "ArrowLeft" || e.key === "ArrowUp"
                    ? Math.max(openStimulus - 1, 0)
                    : e.key === "Home"
                      ? 0
                      : e.key === "End"
                        ? last
                        : null;
              if (to === null) return;
              e.preventDefault();
              setOpenStimulus(to);
              // Focus follows selection, which is what makes the arrows read as
              // moving through the ledger rather than scrubbing something else.
              tickRefs.current[to]?.focus();
            }}
          >
            {stimuli.map((st, i) => {
              const m = st.ungraded ? "ungraded" : st.reciting ? "recited" : BAND[st.band].mark;
              return (
                <button
                  key={st.slug}
                  type="button"
                  className="rc__tick"
                  aria-current={i === openStimulus}
                  tabIndex={i === openStimulus ? 0 : -1}
                  ref={(el) => {
                    tickRefs.current[i] = el;
                  }}
                  onClick={() => setOpenStimulus(i)}
                >
                  {/* The accessible name. The digit below is decoration for a
                      reader who can see the ledger.
                      It used to be the number alone, which made the ledger a
                      row of twenty-two identical buttons to a screen reader:
                      the band and the recital are the whole content of a tick,
                      and both were carried by colour and shape only. */}
                  <span className="sr-only">
                    Stimulus {st.item_order ?? i + 1},{" "}
                    {st.ungraded
                      ? "not marked"
                      : st.reciting
                        ? "said back word for word"
                        : BAND[st.band].label}
                    {i === openStimulus ? ", open" : ""}
                  </span>
                  <span className={cn("rc__mark", `rc__mark--${m}`)} aria-hidden />
                  <span className="rc__tick-n" aria-hidden>
                    {st.item_order ?? i + 1}
                  </span>
                </button>
              );
            })}
          </div>
        </div>
      </header>

      {/* ── The open stimulus ──────────────────────────────────────────── */}

                  Previous
                </button>
                <p className="rc__label">
                  {openStimulus + 1} of {stimuli.length}
                </p>
                <button
                  type="button"
                  className="rc__hear disabled:opacity-40"
                  disabled={openStimulus >= stimuli.length - 1}
                  onClick={() => setOpenStimulus((i) => Math.min(stimuli.length - 1, i + 1))}
                >
                  Next
                </button>
              </div>
            )}
          </div>
        </div>
      )}

      {onStartFocusSet && <FocusSetPanel grade={grade} onStart={onStartFocusSet} />}

      {onDone && (
        <div className="border-t border-[var(--rc-hairline)] px-6 py-6 text-center">
          <button type="button" className="rc__cta" onClick={onDone}>
            Done
          </button>
        </div>
      )}
    </div>
  );
}

     3	/**
     4	 * The four steps, from the one place they are authored.
     5	 *
     6	 * For a while the drill taught three different methods. The door said "Read
     7	 * silently" and the walkthrough said "Read it, out loud in pieces". The door
     8	 * said a colour is "a doubt, a joke" and the walkthrough told her to say "what
     9	 * that piece is doing in the argument, evidence, conclusion", which is exactly
    10	 * the pasteable label the judge marks as no reaction of her own. The card then
    11	 * listed both as faults. She was coached into them by the product and marked
    12	 * down for them by the product.
    13	 *
    14	 * So the step names and their one-line definitions live in the instructions
    15	 * JSON, and every surface reads them from here. If a step is redefined, it is
    16	 * redefined once. `method.test.ts` holds the vocabulary to CONTEXT.md.
    17	 */
    18	export interface MethodStep {
    19	  /** What a student reads. "Read it.", "Tell it back.", "Colour it.", "The Recall." */
    20	  label: string;
    21	  /** The one-line definition, for surfaces too small for the authored body. */
    22	  short: string;
    23	}
    24	
    25	/**
    26	 * The step list, in order.
    27	 *
    28	 * The authored procedure is the one section of the briefing that carries
    29	 * `steps`; there is exactly one, and it has been the four steps since the drill
    30	 * shipped. If a second one is ever authored this throws rather than silently
    31	 * teaching the first four of eight.
    32	 */
    33	export const METHOD_STEPS: readonly MethodStep[] = (() => {
    34	  const withSteps = (instructions.briefing?.sections ?? []).filter((s) => s.steps?.length);
    35	  if (withSteps.length !== 1) {
    36	    throw new Error(
    37	      `read-recall-colour instructions: expected one authored procedure, found ${withSteps.length}`,
    38	    );
    39	  }
    40	  return (withSteps[0].steps ?? []).map((s) => {
    41	    if (!s.short) throw new Error(`method step "${s.label}" has no one-line definition`);
    42	    return { label: s.label, short: s.short };
    43	  });
    44	})();
    45	
    46	/** The label without its authored full stop, for use inside a sentence. */
    47	export function stepName(step: MethodStep): string {
    48	  return step.label.replace(/\.$/, "");
    49	}
    50	
    51	/** Step 4. Named often enough, and mis-named often enough, to be worth a constant. */
    52	export const THE_RECALL = METHOD_STEPS[3];

/* Read, Recall, Colour, section mode.
 *
 * The v1 runner sits on a dark field because it shows one chunk at a time and
 * nothing during recall: there is barely anything on screen to light. A section
 * is the opposite surface. The whole stimulus is present for the whole
 * stimulus, it is the only thing on screen, and she is reading it closely for
 * twenty minutes. That is a document, and a document belongs on paper.
 *
 * So this screen wears the real section's clothes. `OfficialDrillFrame` already
 * mirrors LawHub -- indigo field, white header rail, one centred white card,
 * Fraunces at 19px -- and that is the surface she will sit on test day. Every
 * value below is taken from it rather than invented, and the answering half is
 * removed rather than restyled. No new dialect: this is the drill room.
 *
 * Everything after the frame exists to keep the text in exactly the same place
 * while its state changes.
 */

.ac-section {
  --sec-field: #1a1635;
  --sec-paper: #ffffff;
  --sec-ink: #111827;
  --sec-rule: #cbd5e1;
  --sec-chrome: #334155;
  --sec-warn: #b45309;
  /* The rest of the LawHub slate, named. Thirteen raw literals sat beside
     the six tokens above, which is how a mirror drifts one hex at a time. */
  --sec-rule-2: #e2e8f0;
  --sec-fill: #f1f5f9;
  --sec-paper-2: #f8fafc;
  --sec-ink-2: #475569;
  --sec-ink-3: #64748b;
  --sec-mark: #dbeafe;
  --sec-sans: "Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif;

  display: flex;
  min-height: 100vh;
  flex-direction: column;
  background: var(--sec-field);
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
}

/* ---- the white rail ---------------------------------------------------- */

/* One column per child, and the count has to be kept: declared short once, the
   exit wrapped onto a second row and the rail grew the header from 46px to 96px
   on the drill's only screen. Six now -- position, rail, clock, the recording
   mark, Pause, the way out. */
.ac-section-head {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: auto 1fr auto auto auto auto;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 1.25rem;
  padding: 0.75rem 1.5rem;
  border-bottom: 1px solid var(--sec-rule-2);
  background: var(--sec-paper);
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
}

/* Where she is in the section. The clock sits beside it; see
   `.ac-section-clock` for why it counts up rather than down. */
.ac-section-where {
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

.ac-section-where b {
  font-weight: 600;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
}

.ac-section-rail {
  height: 2px;
  overflow: hidden;
  border-radius: 9999px;
  background: var(--sec-rule-2);
}

.ac-section-rail-fill {
  display: block;
  height: 100%;
  transform-origin: left center;
  background: var(--sec-chrome);
  transition: transform 0.4s cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1);
}

/* Assignment-gated and full screen. Without a way out the only exit is the
   browser's back button, which is a trap rather than a design. */
.ac-section-exit {
  border: none;
  border-radius: 9999px;
  background: none;
  padding: 0.25rem 0.75rem;
  color: inherit;
  font: inherit;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background-color 0.15s ease, color 0.15s ease;
}

.ac-section-exit:hover {
  background: var(--sec-fill);
  color: var(--sec-ink);
}

/* Stepping away, which is the thing that was missing.
 *
 * Same weight as Leave and next to it, because they answer the same question
 * and she should meet them together -- the whole failure this fixes is a
 * student who wanted this one and could only find that one. Deliberately far
 * from the paper: pausing is chrome, not a step in the drill, and nothing that
 * stops the clock may sit where a hand aiming for Space could land on it. */
.ac-section-pause {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-chrome);
  border-radius: 9999px;
  background: none;
  padding: 0.25rem 0.75rem;
  color: inherit;
  font: inherit;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background-color 0.15s ease, color 0.15s ease;
}

.ac-section-pause:hover {
  background: var(--sec-fill);
  color: var(--sec-ink);
}

/* ---- the paper --------------------------------------------------------- */

.ac-section-field {
  display: flex;
  flex: 1;
  padding: 1rem;
}

/* The floor is a floor, not a promise the viewport can keep.
 *
 * `min-height: 700px` was written for a desktop and is taller than a 13-inch
 * laptop has room for. At a 664px viewport the card ran to 778px and pushed the
 * footer -- the instruction, the presence bar and the microphone error, which
....
  font-weight: 400;
  font-optical-sizing: none;
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 14;
}

.ac-section-ready-body {
  max-width: 34ch;
  margin: 0;
  color: var(--sec-ink-3);
  font-size: 0.875rem;
  line-height: 1.65;
}

/* The bar, in the panel, at a size she can see from where she sits. The rail
   version is 34x3 beside a status line; here it is the thing the beat is for,
   and the count does not begin until it has moved. Same element, same ink,
   same rule that it never reads as a score. */
.ac-section-ready-level {
  display: block;
  line-height: 0;
}

.ac-section-ready-level .ac-section-level {
  width: 160px;
  height: 4px;
  margin-left: 0;
}

.ac-section-ready-count {
  /* Was #94a3b8: 2.56:1 at 13px, which is a countdown she cannot read. */
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

/* A break wears the ready beat's clothes on purpose. It is the same kind of
   moment -- the section open, the clock not running, one thing to press -- and
   a second full-card layout for it would be a second thing to keep true. */
.ac-section-ready--paused .ac-section-ready-title {
  color: var(--sec-warn);
}

/* The refused microphone reads as a fault rather than as a beat, so the heading
   takes the warning colour the footer uses for the same class of thing. */
.ac-section-ready[data-mic="refused"] .ac-section-ready-title {
  color: var(--sec-warn);
}

/* Two ways forward, neither of which existed. Asking again leads, because it is
   the one that keeps the section. */
.ac-section-ready-actions {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.75rem;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  justify-content: center;
}

.ac-section-ready-retry,
.ac-section-ready-leave {
  border-radius: 9999px;
  padding: 0.4rem 1.1rem;
  font: inherit;
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background-color 0.15s ease, color 0.15s ease, border-color 0.15s ease;
}

.ac-section-ready-retry {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-ink);
  background: var(--sec-ink);
  color: var(--sec-paper);
}

.ac-section-ready-retry:hover {
  background: var(--sec-chrome);
  border-color: var(--sec-chrome);
}

.ac-section-ready-leave {
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-rule);
  background: none;
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
}

.ac-section-ready-leave:hover {
  background: var(--sec-fill);
  color: var(--sec-ink);
}

@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .ac-section-level-fill {
    transition: none;
  }
}


/* ---- leaving the drill room -------------------------------------------- */

/* The one composed moment in the drill.
 *
 * The runner is a cold indigo mirror of the real section; the Report Card is a
 * violet-cast white on a violet ground. The jump between them was abrupt
 * because there was nothing between them: the field cut to the card in one
 * frame, at the end of twenty-five unbroken minutes, which is the moment she is
 * most likely to remember and the moment that felt most like a page reload.
 *
 * So the field lifts rather than cuts. The paper card holds its position and
 * fades with it, the indigo warms to the ground the Report Card sits on, and
 * the handover happens underneath. Nothing waits on it: the save, the upload
 * and the grading request all run through these nine hundred milliseconds, and
 * the transition is over long before any of them are.
 */
.ac-section.is-finishing {
  /* The ground the card lands on, by value rather than by eye.
     `.rc-ground` (report-card.css) is what the report phase paints, one step
     darker than the card's own `--rc-page` so the card still reads as a card.
     Neither is in scope here, so the value is copied and kept beside the name
     it copies: a change to one is a change to look for in the other.
     It used to warm to the Activity Centre's cream, rgb(244,239,231), which is
     a colour nothing downstream of this moment paints. The field warmed to one
     ground and the card arrived on another, which is the visible step this
     transition exists to remove. */
  background: oklch(0.925 0.014 312);
  transition: background 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
}

.ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-head {
  opacity: 0;
  transition: opacity 500ms ease;
}

.ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-paper {
  border-color: transparent;
  box-shadow: 0 1px 3px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.04);
  transition:
    box-shadow 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1),
    border-color 900ms ease;
}

    18	 * Two rules the drill puts on this screen:

    20	 * - **Nothing is hidden once done.** The regular section picker drops a section
    21	 *   she has sat, which is right when there are two hundred of them and wrong
    22	 *   here, where there are three. Repeating a section is legitimate: she is
    23	 *   training recall, not measuring a score.
    24	 * - **Every Attempt is kept.** Not a count, not the latest. Attempt 1, 2 and 3
    25	 *   each keep their own date and their own Report Card, because the point of

    28	 * Starting is a server call, not an insert. She chooses the section; the row

    51	function attemptDate(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): string {
    52	  const when = new Date(a.completed_at ?? a.created_at);
    53	  return when.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: "numeric", month: "short" });
    54	}
    55	
    56	/**
    57	 * A section she was handed and has not opened.
    58	 *
    59	 * Not an Attempt: nothing was recorded, nothing was answered, and calling it
    60	 * "Attempt 1 - not finished" tells her she abandoned a run she never began.
    61	 * `started_at` is the honest test, because a teacher-assigned row and a
    62	 * server-made one both arrive as rows and only one of them has a clock.
    63	 */
    64	function isWaiting(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): boolean {
    65	  return a.status === "assigned" && !a.started_at;

    76	/**

    79	 * "Still being marked" is true at four minutes and true at four hours, and she
    80	 * cannot tell those apart, so she either sits waiting on something that is
    81	 * already broken or writes off something that was about to land. A real
    82	 * grading takes one to three minutes; anything much past that is worth her
    83	 * knowing as a number.
    84	 */
    85	function markingMinutes(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): number | null {
    86	  if (!a.completed_at) return null;
    87	  const ms = Date.now() - new Date(a.completed_at).getTime();
    88	  if (!Number.isFinite(ms) || ms < 0) return null;
    89	  return Math.floor(ms / 60000);
    90	}
    91	
    92	/** The clock time a run finished, for a wait long enough that minutes stop helping. */
    93	function finishedAtTime(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): string | null {

    96	    hour: "numeric",
    97	    minute: "2-digit",
    98	  });
    99	}

   101	function attemptLabel(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary, grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined): string {
   102	  const date = attemptDate(a);

   104	  if (!grade) {

   106	    if (waited === null) return `${date} · still being marked`;
   107	    if (waited >= 60) {
   108	      const at = finishedAtTime(a);
   109	      return at ? `${date} · finished ${at}, still being marked` : `${date} · still being marked`;

   111	    if (waited < 1) return `${date} · marking now`;
   112	    return `${date} · marking for ${waited} ${waited === 1 ? "minute" : "minutes"}`;
