   145	}
   146	
   147	export function SectionLibrary({
   148	  attempts,
   149	  onStart,
   150	  onOpenAttempt,
   151	  loadChoices = listSectionChoices,
   152	  loadGrades = listMySectionGrades,
   153	  beginAttempt = startSectionAttempt,
   154	}: SectionLibraryProps) {
   155	  const [choices, setChoices] = useState<SectionChoice[] | null>(null);
   156	  /**
   157	   * Her Report Cards, by assignment.
   158	   *
   159	   * Loaded beside the sections and never waited on: the list is useful without
   160	   * them, and a failed read costs a number on a row rather than the screen.
   161	   */
   162	  const [grades, setGrades] = useState<Map<string, SectionGradeStub>>(new Map());
   163	  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
   164	  const [starting, setStarting] = useState<string | null>(null);
   165	
   166	  useEffect(() => {
   167	    let cancelled = false;
   168	    void (async () => {
   169	      try {
   170	        const rows = await loadChoices();
   171	        if (!cancelled) setChoices(rows);
   172	      } catch {
   173	        // Named plainly. An empty list and a failed read are different facts,
   174	        // and only one of them means "nothing to do tonight".
   175	        if (!cancelled) setError("Could not load the sections. Try again in a moment.");
   176	      }
   177	    })();
   178	    return () => {
   179	      cancelled = true;
   180	    };
   181	  }, [loadChoices]);
   182	
   183	  useEffect(() => {
   184	    let cancelled = false;
   185	    void (async () => {
   186	      try {
   187	        const rows = await loadGrades();
   188	        if (cancelled) return;
   189	        // Newest Attempt per assignment. `listMySectionGrades` returns them
   190	        // newest first, so the first one seen for an assignment wins.
   191	        const byAssignment = new Map<string, SectionGradeStub>();
   192	        for (const g of rows) if (!byAssignment.has(g.assignment_id)) byAssignment.set(g.assignment_id, g);
   193	        setGrades(byAssignment);
   194	      } catch {
   195	        // A missing number is a missing number. The list still works.
   196	      }
   197	    })();
   198	    return () => {
   199	      cancelled = true;
   200	    };
   201	  }, [loadGrades]);
   202	
   203	  /** Her attempts, newest first, grouped by which section they were on. */
   204	  const bySection = useMemo(() => {
   205	    const map = new Map<string, ActivityAssignmentSummary[]>();
   206	    for (const a of attempts) {
   207	      if (a.mode !== "section" || !a.source_section) continue;
   208	      // A row she has never opened is not a run. See `waitingBySection`.
   209	      if (isWaiting(a)) continue;
   210	      const list = map.get(a.source_section) ?? [];
   211	      list.push(a);
   212	      map.set(a.source_section, list);
   213	    }
   214	    for (const list of map.values()) {
   215	      list.sort((x, y) => new Date(y.created_at).getTime() - new Date(x.created_at).getTime());
   216	    }
   217	    return map;
   218	  }, [attempts]);
   219	
   220	  /**
   221	   * Sections Joshua handed her that she has not opened yet.
   222	   *
   223	   * These carry their own message and their own classroom link, so the row IS
   224	   * her assignment rather than a spare copy of one.
   225	   */
   226	  const waitingBySection = useMemo(() => {
   227	    const map = new Map<string, ActivityAssignmentSummary>();
   228	    for (const a of attempts) {
   229	      if (a.mode !== "section" || !a.source_section || !isWaiting(a)) continue;
   230	      const held = map.get(a.source_section);
   231	      // Oldest wins: it is the one whose message she was actually sent.
   232	      if (!held || new Date(a.created_at) < new Date(held.created_at)) {
   233	        map.set(a.source_section, a);
   234	      }
   235	    }
   236	    return map;
   237	  }, [attempts]);
   238	
   239	  const start = useCallback(
   240	    async (choice: SectionChoice) => {
   241	      /*
   242	       * Open the row she was given, rather than making a second one.
   243	       *
   244	       * `start_section_attempt` DELETES a never-started row for the same
   245	       * section before inserting its own. On 2026-09-08 eight sections were
   246	       * assigned to Yahunathan one row at a time, each with its own message and
   247	       * classroom link; pressing Start here would have deleted the row those
   248	       * links point at and left him with "Activity not found." Her assignment
   249	       * and her Attempt are the same row, so this opens it.
   250	       */
   251	      const waiting = waitingBySection.get(choice.source_section);
   252	      if (waiting) {
   253	        onStart(waiting.id);
   254	        return;
   255	      }
   256	      setStarting(choice.id);
   257	      setError(null);
   258	      try {
   259	        onStart(await beginAttempt(choice.id));
   260	      } catch {
   261	        setError("Could not start that section. Try again in a moment.");
   262	        setStarting(null);
   263	      }
   264	    },
   265	    [beginAttempt, onStart, waitingBySection],
   266	  );
   267	
   268	  if (error && !choices) {
   269	    return <p className="sl-note">{error}</p>;
   270	  }
   271	  if (!choices) {
   272	    return <p className="sl-note">Loading sections\u2026</p>;
   273	  }
   274	  /*
   275	   * An empty list is a fact, not a blank space.
   276	   *
   277	   * "Choose a section" carries her to this slab, and a slab with nothing in it
   278	   * is the dead end she reports as the drill being broken. Say what is true:
   279	   * nothing has been built for her yet.
   280	   */
   281	  if (choices.length === 0) {
   282	    return (
   283	      <p className="sl-note">
   284	        No sections are ready for you yet. Joshua adds them here as they are built.
   285	        Check back, or message him if you were expecting one tonight.
   286	      </p>
   287	    );
   288	  }
   289	
   290	  return (
   291	    <div className="sl-sections">
   292	      {error && (
   293	        <p className="sl-note" data-tone="bad">
   294	          {error}
   295	        </p>
   296	      )}
   297	      {choices.map((choice) => {
   298	        const done = bySection.get(choice.source_section) ?? [];
   299	        return (
   300	          <div key={choice.id} className="sl-section" data-section={choice.source_section}>
   301	            <div className="sl-sec-body">
   302	              <div className="sl-sec-line">
   303	                <span className="sl-sec-name">{choice.source_section}</span>
   304	                {/*
   305	                  * The time, not the stimulus count.
   306	                  *
   307	                  * A stimulus is our grain, not hers, and the counts differ per
   308	                  * section for a reason she has no way to see: two questions can
   309	                  * share a stimulus. Printed on a row to choose from, "22" next
   310	                  * to "26" reads as one section being longer, which is not what
   311	                  * it means. The clock is the thing she actually plans around.
   312	                  */}
   313	                <span className="sl-sec-clock">
   314	                  {choice.goal_minutes?.standard ?? 25} min
   315	                </span>
   316	              </div>
   317	
   318	              {done.length === 0 ? (
   319	                <p className="sl-note">Not run yet.</p>
   320	              ) : (
   321	                /*
   322	                 * Every Attempt, and every one of them a way back in.
   323	                 *
   324	                 * The Report Card used to exist only in the tab that produced it:
   325	                 * this list rendered the same runs as plain text, so a grade she
   326	                 * could not have seen -- grading finishes minutes after she stops
   327	                 * -- was unreachable for ever after. A finished Attempt opens on
   328	                 * its own Report Card; an unfinished one is not a run and does
   329	                 * not pretend to lead anywhere.
   330	                 */
   331	                <ul className="sl-sec-attempts">
   332	                  {done.map((a, i) => {
   333	                    const grade = grades.get(a.id);
   334	                    const label = `Attempt ${done.length - i} \u00b7 ${attemptLabel(a, grade)}`;
   335	                    const action = attemptAction(a, grade);
   336	                    const reachable = a.status === "completed" && onOpenAttempt !== undefined;
   337	                    return (
   338	                      <li key={a.id}>
   339	                        {reachable ? (
   340	                          <button
   341	                            type="button"
   342	                            className="sl-sec-attempt"
   343	                            onClick={() => onOpenAttempt?.(a.id)}
   344	                          >
   345	                            <span className="sl-sec-attempt-label">{label}</span>
   346	                            {action && <span className="sl-sec-attempt-go">{action}</span>}
   347	                          </button>
   348	                        ) : (
   349	                          <span className="sl-sec-attempt" data-flat="true">
   350	                            <span className="sl-sec-attempt-label">{label}</span>
   351	                            {action && <span className="sl-sec-attempt-go">{action}</span>}
   352	                          </span>
   353	                        )}
   354	                      </li>
   355	                    );
   356	                  })}
   357	                </ul>
   358	              )}
   359	            </div>
   360	
   361	            <button
   362	              type="button"
   363	              className="sl-sec-go"
   364	              disabled={starting !== null}
   365	              onClick={() => void start(choice)}
   366	            >
   367	              {starting === choice.id ? "Starting\u2026" : done.length > 0 ? "Run it again" : "Start"}
   368	            </button>
   369	          </div>
   370	        );
   371	      })}
   372	    </div>
   373	  );
   374	}

Tool result:
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 96;
  font-weight: 380;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
  line-height: 1.02;
}

/* ── The console ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */

.rc__console {
  background: var(--rc-console);
  color: var(--rc-console-ink);
}

.rc__console-divider {
  border-color: var(--rc-console-hairline);
}

/* ── The ledger of twenty-two ─────────────────────────────────────────────
   The whole section as one row of marks, so the shape of the run is visible
   before any one stimulus is opened. Twenty-two accordion rows are a list;
   this is a picture. */
.rc__ledger {
  display: grid;
  gap: 4px;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(28px, 1fr));
}

.rc__tick {
  align-items: center;
  background: transparent;
  border: 0;
  border-radius: 6px;
  cursor: pointer;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 5px;
  /* 44px of target, reached by being 44px rather than by wrapped text. */
  min-height: 44px;
  justify-content: center;
  padding: 4px 0;
  transition: background-color 160ms var(--rc-ease);
}

.rc__tick:hover {
  background: var(--rc-console-raised);
}

.rc__tick:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rc-signal-bright);
  outline-offset: 2px;
}

.rc__tick-n {
  color: var(--rc-console-ink-faint);
  font-family: var(--rc-mono);
  font-size: 9px;
  letter-spacing: 0.04em;
  transition: color 160ms var(--rc-ease);
}

.rc__tick[aria-current="true"] .rc__tick-n {
  color: var(--rc-console-ink);
}

/* The mark itself. FILL carries the band, so the ledger survives grayscale.
   The fill is gold rather than white because gold is what she held on this
   card, and a row of twenty-two white squares is a barcode. */
.rc__mark {
  border: 1.5px solid var(--rc-console-ink-dim);
  border-radius: 3px;
  display: block;
  height: 18px;
  position: relative;
  width: 18px;
}

/* Held everything: solid. */
.rc__mark--at-the-standard {
  background: var(--rc-colour-lit);
  border-color: var(--rc-colour-lit);
}

/* Most of it: filled from the floor, so the gap at the top is what she lost. */
.rc__mark--nearly-there::after,
.rc__mark--thin::after {
  background: var(--rc-colour-lit);
  bottom: 0;
  content: "";
  left: 0;
  position: absolute;
  right: 0;
}

.rc__mark--nearly-there::after {
  height: 66%;
}

.rc__mark--thin::after {
  height: 30%;
}

/* Lost it: a hollow ring. Empty, and round against three squares, so it reads
   as a different kind of thing rather than as a worse colour. */
.rc__mark--lost-it {
  border-color: var(--rc-detail-lit);
  border-radius: 50%;
}

/* Said back word for word: an open mark with a bar through it. Not a worse
   score, which is the whole point, so it takes no hue at all. */
.rc__mark--recited {
  border-radius: 50%;
  border-style: dashed;
}

.rc__mark--ungraded {
  border-style: dotted;
  border-color: var(--rc-console-ink-faint);
}

/* The stimulus currently open, marked on the ledger so she never loses her
   place in twenty-two. */
.rc__tick[aria-current="true"] .rc__mark {
  box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px var(--rc-console), 0 0 0 3.5px var(--rc-signal-bright);
}

/* ── The transport ────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */

/* The card's own primary control. Done, Check again, and Run these again
   used to be the corridor's shadcn Button, which took the shell's emerald into
   a world whose brief vetoes it. Console ink on the console colour, 44px, the
   same pill as every other control in here. */
.rc__cta {
  align-items: center;
  background: var(--rc-console);
  border: 1px solid var(--rc-console);
  border-radius: 999px;
  color: var(--rc-console-ink);
  cursor: pointer;
  display: inline-flex;
  font-family: var(--rc-body);
  font-size: 14px;
  font-weight: 500;
  justify-content: center;
  min-height: 44px;
  padding: 0 22px;
  transition:
    background-color 180ms var(--rc-ease),
    border-color 180ms var(--rc-ease),
    color 180ms var(--rc-ease);
}
.rc__cta:hover:not(:disabled) {
  background: var(--rc-ink);
  border-color: var(--rc-ink);
}
.rc__cta:disabled {
  cursor: default;
  opacity: 0.6;
}
.rc__cta--quiet {
  background: transparent;
  border-color: var(--rc-hairline);
  color: var(--rc-ink);
}
.rc__cta--quiet:hover:not(:disabled) {
  background: transparent;
  border-color: var(--rc-ink);
}

.rc__transport {
  align-items: center;
  background: var(--rc-console-raised);
  border-radius: 999px;
  display: inline-flex;
  gap: 2px;
  padding: 3px;
}

.rc__source {
  background: transparent;
  border: 0;
  border-radius: 999px;
  color: var(--rc-console-ink-dim);
  cursor: pointer;
  font-family: var(--rc-body);
  font-size: 12px;
  font-weight: 500;
  /* PRODUCT.md binds 44x44 by being that size. These were 34. */
  min-height: 44px;
  padding: 0 14px;
  transition:
    background-color 180ms var(--rc-ease),
    color 180ms var(--rc-ease);
  white-space: nowrap;
}

.rc__source:hover {
  color: var(--rc-console-ink);
}

.rc__source[aria-pressed="true"] {
  background: var(--rc-signal);
  color: var(--rc-signal-ink);
}

.rc__source:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rc-signal-bright);
  outline-offset: 2px;
}

 * footer -- the instruction, the presence bar and the microphone error, which
 * are the only words on the screen telling her what to do and whether she is
 * being heard -- 55px below the fold. She would never see "Nothing is reaching
 * the microphone" on that machine.
 *
 * The `max-width: 640px` rule below did zero the floor, but it is a WIDTH
 * query, and the laptop that fails this is 1440 wide. Height is what was short,
 * so height is what the cap is written against.
 *
 * `min()` rather than a smaller constant, so the same bug cannot return at
 * 600px: the card asks for 700 and takes whatever the viewport can actually
 * spare, less the header and the field's own padding. On anything taller than
 * about 800px nothing changes at all. */
.ac-section-paper {
  display: flex;
  width: 100%;
  max-width: 1400px;
  min-height: min(700px, calc(100vh - 7rem));
  margin: 0 auto;
  flex-direction: column;
  overflow: hidden;
  border: 1px solid var(--sec-rule);
  border-radius: 0.375rem;
  background: var(--sec-paper);
  box-shadow: 0 4px 18px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.35);
}

/* The stimulus is the only thing in the card, so its column is centred
   horizontally -- but it hangs from the top, exactly as it does in the column it
   used to share with a question.
 *
 * Centring it vertically was the first cut and it was wrong: stimuli run from
 * two lines to nine, so a centred block starts at a different height on every
 * one of the 23 and the whole page appears to jump between them. The spec bans
 * reflow inside a stimulus; this is the same failure one level up. */
.ac-section-body {
  display: flex;
  flex: 1;
  /* A flex item will not shrink below its content unless it is told it may. The
     stimulus is the part that can be scrolled past; the footer is not, so the
     reading area is what gives way when the card is short. */
  min-height: 0;
  align-items: flex-start;
  justify-content: center;
  overflow-y: auto;
  padding: 4rem 1.5rem 3rem;
}

.ac-section-stimulus {
  max-width: 52ch;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
  font-family: Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif;
  font-size: 19px;
  font-weight: 400;
  line-height: 1.7;
  /* Fraunces is a variable font with an optical-size axis. Left on `auto` the
     browser picks the cut from the rendered size; pin it to the text-optimised
     end so the hairlines stay put. Grayscale smoothing thins them too. */
  font-optical-sizing: none;
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 14;
  -webkit-font-smoothing: subpixel-antialiased;

## user

Tool result:
     * a violet-cast white on a violet ground. `is-finishing` warms the field
     * and lifts the chrome over nine hundred milliseconds so the card arrives
     * on ground that is already the right colour, which means the colour the
     * report phase actually paints (`.rc-ground`) and not the Activity
     * Centre's cream. It is decoration and it is deliberately on the one frame
     * she is most likely to remember.
     */
    return (
      <div className="ac-section is-finishing">
        <main className="ac-section-field">
          <div className="ac-section-paper">
            <div className="ac-section-body">
              <p className="ac-section-finished">
                Section finished.
                <span>
                  {unsavedCount > 0 && uploadFailed
                    ? `${unsavedCount} ${unsavedCount === 1 ? "answer" : "answers"} did not reach us. Your recording did not reach us either, so both are still on this computer. Open this section again from the same browser and they will be sent, and then marked.`
                    : unsavedCount > 0 && recordingStored
                      ? `${unsavedCount} ${unsavedCount === 1 ? "answer" : "answers"} did not reach us, but the recording did. Open this section again from the same browser and they will be sent, and then marked.`
                      : unsavedCount > 0
                        ? `${unsavedCount} ${unsavedCount === 1 ? "answer" : "answers"} did not reach us, and there was no recording of this run to mark instead. Open this section again from the same browser and the answers will be sent.`
                        : uploadFailed
                          ? `${run.stimuli.length} stimuli, end to end. Your recording did not reach us, so it is still on this computer. Open this section again from the same browser and it will be sent, and then marked.`
                          : !recordingStored
                            ? `${run.stimuli.length} stimuli, end to end. Nothing was recorded of this run, so there is no audio to mark and none of it is on this computer either. Tell Joshua what happened and he can see what was saved.`
                            : `${run.stimuli.length} stimuli, end to end. Your Report Card comes next.`}
                </span>
              </p>
              {(unsavedCount > 0 || !recordingStored) && onExit && (
                <button type="button" className="ac-section-exit" onClick={onExit}>
                  Back to the Activity Centre
                </button>
              )}
            </div>
          </div>
        </main>
      </div>
    );
  }

  return (
    <SectionStage
      stimulus={stimulus}
      phase={cursor.phase}
      stimulusNumber={cursor.item + 1}
      totalStimuli={run.stimuli.length}
      /*
       * A refused microphone is the loud fault and wins the line. A live one
       * that heard nothing through a whole Recall is the quiet one, and it is
       * the one that otherwise ends in a perfect-looking run and a card of
       * zeros.
       */
      error={
        // While the beat is held on a refusal, the panel is already saying it
        // in full. Repeating it in the footer is not emphasis, it is noise.
        countdown !== null && micRefused
          ? null
          : // A break is not a fault, and the dead-input line was measured
            // against a window she was not in the room for.
            paused
            ? null
            : (micError ??
            (deadInput
              ? "Nothing is reaching the microphone. Check it is not muted, then keep going."
              : null))
      }
      onExit={onExit ? askToExit : undefined}
      sectionName={run.sourceSection}
      /* What the elapsed figure is elapsed against. Her goal, not the standard
         one: an accommodation scales it, and pacing her against someone else's
         is worse than showing no goal at all. */
      goalSeconds={run.timeGoalMinutes[accommodation] * 60}
      elapsedSeconds={countdown === null ? elapsed : 0}
      /* "Recording" must not be on the rail while nothing is being written to
         the file. The paused mark takes its place. */
      recording={recording && !micRefused && !paused}
      paused={paused}
      /* No pausing before the section has started -- there is nothing to pause
         and nothing to lose -- and none once it is over. */
      onPause={countdown === null ? pauseRun : undefined}
      onResume={resumeRun}
      pauseError={pauseError}
      getStream={getStream}
      onDeadInput={setDeadInput}
      onHeard={() => setHeard(true)}
      /* The beat says which of its two states it is in: still waiting to hear
         her, or counting because it has. */
      micHeard={heard}
      micChecked={heard || checkExpired}
      micRefused={micRefused}
      onRetryMic={startRecording}
      confirmingExit={confirmingExit}
      onConfirmExit={onExit}
      onCancelExit={() => setConfirmingExit(false)}
      countdown={countdown}
      /* The same act as the Space handler, for a device with no Space key. The
         stage decides whether to draw it; the runner only supplies the act. */
      onAdvance={press}
      /* First section only, first three stimuli only, and never in the loop. */
      coach={coachOn ? coachNote(cursor.item, cursor.phase) : null}
      onDismissCoach={() => setCoachOn(false)}
    />
  );
}

---
REPORT CARD · ATTEMPT 2
At the standard

15 of 22 at the standard, 3 with nothing dropped.

The move is what an argument runs on: the loophole it leaves, the inference it forces, the conflict it sets up. Holding a stimulus is Coverage. Naming the move is The Read.

HELD

82%

of what was on the page, said back in your own words

UNDERSTOOD

15 of 22

where you found the move. On 3 you did not give a Read at all.

TIME

28:32

against a 25 minute goal

Over the goal, which early runs are meant to be. The goal is what the drill costs once it is a habit, not a limit on today.

WORTH WORKING ON
1 said back close to word for word
1 told as a list rather than an argument
3 with no reaction of your own
Longest freeze inside a recall: 7s
You read 2 of them out loud before recalling. That is not marked against you, and reading silently is the harder, better version.
STIMULUS BY STIMULUS
Stimulus 1, At the standard, open

STIMULUS 1 OF 22
At the standard

95% held. Almost all of it came back.

Hear it back

THE PASSAGE
WHAT YOU HELD
One line came back whole.
Your reaction was about this stimulus, not one that would fit any of them.
WHAT SLIPPED
all employees
EVERYTHING ELSE THAT SLIPPED (1)
YOUR READ

You gave a Read, but not the one this argument turns on.

She said the consultant may be assuming everyone wants to move, which is a different move from the one the argument turns on.

The Read this one turns on: What if exploit is doing two jobs in this argument?

LINE BY LINE

1. Ace Manufacturing's consultant proposes that we reassign staff so that all employees are doing both what they like to do and what they do well.

Hear it

so the director says their consultant wants people moved around so everyone is doing what they like and what they are good at

Dropped that it applies to all employees. You said everyone, which is close, but the staff being reassigned was every employee.

HELD
A consultant is proposing this.
Staff would be reassigned.
People would do what they like and what they do well.
DROPPED
This applies to all employees.
DONE RIGHT

So a management consultant is telling us how to run the company, and the idea is to move every single employee so they are doing the things they enjoy and the things they are good at.

2. This, she says, will "increase productivity by fully exploiting our available resources."

Hear it

and she thinks that makes the place more productive because you get the most out of everyone

HELD
This is the conclusion.
Productivity would increase.
The reason is fully exploiting resources.
DONE RIGHT

And her claim is that this raises productivity, because you would be getting everything you can out of the people you already have.

The whole thing, text off screen: partly there.

The recall kept the proposal and the claim, and lost the quantifier again.

WHAT YOU SAID

so the director says their consultant wants people moved around so that everyone is doing what they like and what they are actually good at, and she reckons that gets more out of the place

The marking is done from these words. If they are not what you said, the marking is wrong, not you. Play it back and say so.

This is not what I said

NEXT TIME

Say the quantifier on purpose as you read: all employees, not the staff. It is the one word this argument leans on.

Previous

1 OF 22

Next
Done

.sl-label {
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 11px;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}

/* =============================================================== hero */

.rd-hero {
  padding: 14px 14px 0;
}
.rd-hero-panel {
  position: relative;
...
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  padding: 22px 28px 0;
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 11px;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: rgba(var(--rd-on-dark-rgb), 0.78);
}
.rd-crumbs strong {
  font-weight: 500;
  color: var(--rd-on-dark);
}
.rd-crumb-sep {
  margin: 0 10px;
  opacity: 0.55;
}
.rd-meta {
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}
.sl-crumb-back {
  position: relative;
  font: inherit;
  letter-spacing: inherit;
  text-transform: inherit;
  color: inherit;
  background: none;
  border: 0;
  padding: 0;
  cursor: pointer;
  text-decoration: underline;
  text-decoration-style: dotted;
...
.rd-demo-foot {
  margin: 0;
  padding: 8px 14px;
  border-top: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 10px;
  letter-spacing: 0.08em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}

/* =============================================================== band */

.rd-band {
  display: grid;
  gap: 14px;
  max-width: 1240px;
  margin: 28px auto 0;
  padding: 0 24px;
}
.sl-notice {
  margin: 0;
  padding: 16px 20px;
  background: var(--rd-paper);
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
  border-radius: 16px;
  font-size: 15px;
  line-height: 1.55;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}

/* ============================================================= method */

...
.rd-count {
  position: relative;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  padding: 26px 26px 28px;
  background: var(--rd-paper);
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
  border-radius: var(--rd-radius);
}
.rd-count[data-tone="good"] {
  background: var(--rd-indigo);
  border-color: var(--rd-indigo);
  color: var(--rd-on-dark);
  box-shadow: 0 34px 60px -34px rgba(var(--rd-ink-rgb), 0.7);
}
.rd-count-tag {
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 11px;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}
.rd-count[data-tone="good"] .rd-count-tag {
  color: var(--rd-apricot-light);
}
.rd-count-quote {
  margin: 18px 0 0;
  font-family: var(--rd-display);
  font-size: 22px;
  line-height: 1.32;
  letter-spacing: -0.012em;
  text-wrap: pretty;
}
.rd-count-note {
  margin: auto 0 0;
  padding-top: 20px;
  font-size: 14.5px;
  line-height: 1.5;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
.rd-count[data-tone="good"] .rd-count-note {
  color: rgba(var(--rd-on-dark-rgb), 0.82);
}

/* =============================================================== slab */

.sl-runs {
  display: grid;
...
.sl-note {
  margin: 0;
  font-size: 14px;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}
.sl-note[data-tone="bad"] {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-weight: 500;
}

...
.sl-stats {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(4, minmax(0, 1fr));
  background: var(--rd-paper);
  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
  border-radius: var(--rd-radius);
  overflow: hidden;
}
.sl-stat {
  padding: 20px 22px 22px;
  border-left: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
}
.sl-stat:first-child {
  border-left: 0;
}
.sl-sv {
  display: block;
  margin-top: 8px;
  font-family: var(--rd-display);
  font-weight: 400;
  font-size: 34px;
  line-height: 1;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
.sl-sv small {
  font-size: 16px;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}
.sl-sv[data-dir="down"] {
  color: var(--rd-emerald);
}
.sl-sv[data-empty] {
  font-family: var(--rd-sans);
  font-size: 15px;
  font-weight: 500;
  letter-spacing: 0;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}
.sl-sd {
  display: block;
  margin-top: 6px;
  font-size: 13px;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

/* ---- her runs ---- */
.sl-scroll {
  overflow-x: auto;
  margin-top: 14px;
}
.sl-rtable {
  width: 100%;
  border-collapse: collapse;
  font-size: 14px;
}
.sl-rtable th {
  text-align: left;
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 10.5px;
  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  font-weight: 400;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
  padding: 0 12px 10px 0;
}
.sl-rtable th.sl-r,
.sl-rtable td.sl-r {
  text-align: right;
  padding-right: 0;
}
.sl-rtable td {
  padding: 12px 12px 12px 0;
  border-top: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  white-space: nowrap;
}
.sl-rtable td:first-child {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-weight: 500;
}
.sl-ticks {
  display: inline-flex;
  gap: 3px;
  justify-content: flex-end;
}
.sl-tick {
  width: 5px;
  height: 16px;
  border-radius: 2px;
  background: var(--rd-emerald);
}
.sl-tick[data-state="notJudged"] {
  background: var(--rd-paper);
  box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--rd-emerald);
}
.sl-tick[data-state="silent"] {
  background: var(--rd-line);
  box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--rd-ink-3);
}
.sl-tick[data-state="missing"] {
  background: var(--rd-line);
}

{
  "task": "Work through the stimulus out loud, a piece at a time. When you have got all of it, press space. The text goes away, and you say the entire argument back from memory.",
  "coach": {
    "drill": "a whole Logical Reasoning section run out loud, where the student reads each line of a stimulus, looks away, tells it back from memory in her own words, reacts to it, and gives The Recall of the whole stimulus before moving to the next one",
    "rubric": [
      "Judge by meaning, never by wording. Her own words are required here, so a good recall will never match the line's phrasing. High overlap with the original is the failure, not the standard.",
      "Order is free. She is encouraged to deliver the ideas out of the order she read them in, and reordering must never cost her anything.",
      "Casual, unpolished, laughing delivery is expected. Never mark down for style, length or fluency.",
      "A detail merely gestured at is not carried. Naming the topic is not the same as saying what was claimed about it.",
      "Numbers, names, dates, quantities and connecting words are each their own detail. Never treat one as carried because the sentence around it was.",
      "A reaction that could be pasted onto any other sentence does not count as a colour. Judge whether it engages this content, never whether it is correct or well put.",
      "Score The Read against the captured key for that stimulus, not against a retelling. A different name for the same move is fine; missing the move is not.",
      "Silence during a silent read is correct and expected. Only silence inside a recall or a colour counts against her."
    ],
    "partial_rule": "Mark 'partial' when the retelling is real but at least one detail went missing, which is the drill's ordinary state. Mark 'incorrect' only when the response is a gist or a topic label rather than a retelling."
  },
  "rules": [
    "Everything here is out loud. The whole section records in one take. You can pause: the text hides and the clock stops while you are away, and you cannot go back to a stimulus.",
    "Read silently, about a sentence at a time. When you are not sure how much to take on, take less.",
    "Look away and tell it back in your own words. Then react to it. Both out loud.",
    "Keep going like that through the whole stimulus.",
    "Press space when you have got all of it. The stimulus vanishes and stays gone. Now say the whole argument back from memory, with every detail you can hold.",
    "Press space again and the next one loads. No going back, all the way to the end."
  ],
  "tagline": "Read it, tell it back, react to it. Then the stimulus disappears and you say the whole argument back from memory.",
  "briefing": {
    "lede": "Worth reading properly once. After that, skim the rules above and go.",
    "closer": "One a day holds you steady. The second one is where you improve.",
    "sections": [
      {
        "body": [
          "You follow a stimulus, sentence by sentence, and it all makes sense as it goes. Then you get down to answer choice C and realise you are holding almost none of it. That is not a comprehension problem. It is a retention problem, and it is the one this drill was built for.",
          "Understanding is necessary for everything and sufficient for nothing. If the argument is not still with you while you work the answers, your understanding never got the chance to help.",
          "Most of what you call reading is built for getting to the end. Scanning names. Dropping the connecting words. Turning a list of three into one label. That is a fine way to read a phone bill. It is a bad way to read something that is about to ask you for every detail back."
        ],
        "heading": "You can understand every sentence and still arrive with nothing."
      },
      {
        "body": [
          "Three steps through the stimulus, then a fourth step once you have read all of it. You decide how much to take on at a time, and nothing on the screen is tracking where you are.",
          "Space is the only key, and you press it twice per stimulus. The first press takes the text away and asks you for the whole argument from memory. The second press moves you to the next stimulus. Nothing advances on its own, so a pause to think costs you nothing but the clock."
        ],
        "steps": [
          {
            "body": "Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.",
            "label": "Read it.",
            "short": "Read about a sentence at a time, silently. Nothing is spoken yet."
          },
          {
            "body": "Look away from the screen and tell it back in your own words, out loud, like you are telling a friend what it said. Out of order is fine. Casual is fine. Laughing is fine.",
            "label": "Tell it back.",
            "short": "Look away and tell it back in your own words, out loud."
          },
          {
            "body": "React. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke, a feeling. One or two seconds is plenty. It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours.",
            "label": "Colour it.",
            "short": "React to it out loud. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke."
          },
          {
            "body": "Press space. The stimulus disappears and does not come back. Now say the entire argument back from memory, out loud, with every detail you can still hold. This is the part that gets scored.",
            "label": "The Recall.",
            "short": "The text goes. Say the whole argument back from memory."
          }
        ],
        "heading": "What you actually do."
      },
      {
        "body": [
          "Do not give back the gist, or the summary, or the important parts. You cannot know which parts were important until you have read the whole thing, so filtering on the way through mostly means quietly throwing out the piece you were going to need.",
          "Fewer words is fine when the words cover the same ideas. Fewer ideas is not. The names, the numbers, and the small words that carry the load (all, most, only, probably) either come back or they do not.",
          "**Efficiency is never a licence to leave information out.**"
        ],
        "heading": "Simpler is not the same as shorter."
      },
      {
        "body": [
          "You will open your mouth to colour something and find nothing there. That lasts about a week.",
          "It is not that you have no thoughts. It is that nobody has ever asked you for one in the middle of reading before, and the habit takes a few sessions to wake up. Say something obvious on purpose if you have to. \"Okay, sure.\" \"That seems like a stretch.\" \"Who measured that?\" All of those count.",
          "The reaction is what makes the sentence yours instead of the author's. Skip it and you are back to repeating words you do not own."
        ],
        "heading": "You will think you have nothing to say. Everybody does."
      },
      {
        "body": [
          "While you are working the stimulus, nothing on the screen hides. The whole thing stays readable, which means the only thing stopping you rereading a line while you tell it back is you, looking away from it.",
          "That is deliberate. Reading while you recall is a one way street with cars coming both ways. It jams. You stop using your memory at all and slide straight back into the author's exact words instead of finding your own.",
          "It is also the version that still works on paper, and on test day. A screen that hides the text for you is a crutch that cannot come with you."
        ],
        "heading": "Look away. That part is on you."
      },
      {
        "body": [
          "The goal is twenty-five minutes for a whole section, and it only counts if every stimulus got recalled, coloured, and given The Recall. Finishing early with four of them skipped is not finishing early. If you have extra time on test day, your goal scales with it.",
          "The clock in the corner shows how long you have been going. It is there so you can pace a section. It never counts down, because a number falling away in the corner of your eye produces exactly the skating read this drill exists to fix.",
          "It is not a race. It is the pace at which this stops being a study exercise and starts being how you read under a clock. Go slower while it is new. The speed shows up on its own once the method does."
        ],
        "heading": "Twenty-five minutes, and what that number means."
      }
    ]
  }
}

import { useMemo, useRef } from "react";
import { motion, useInView, useReducedMotion, useTransform, type MotionValue } from "framer-motion";
/**
 * The dusk meadow behind the Read, Recall, Colour door.
 *
 * Painted, not photographed, and painted at render time: every ridge is a
 * seeded sum of sines, every blade of grass a seeded quadratic, so the scene
 * ships as geometry rather than as three PNGs that have to arrive. It is one
 * picture cut into five depth planes -- sky, far hills, mid hills, near hills,
 * foreground grass -- and each plane is its own layer so the door can move them
 * at different rates as she scrolls. The demo card sits BETWEEN the near hills
 * and the grass, which is what makes the grass pass in front of it.
 *
 * Palette is the runner's own indigo field lifted into a sky, so the door and
 * the drill room read as one evening.
 */
const W = 1600;
const H = 900;
function mulberry32(seed: number) {
  let t = seed;
  return () => {
    t += 0x6d2b79f5;
    let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);
    r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);
    return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
  };
}
/** A smooth ridge: a few sines summed, closed to the bottom edge. */
function ridge(seed: number, base: number, amp: number): string {
  const rng = mulberry32(seed);
  const waves = Array.from({ length: 4 }, (_, i) => ({
    f: (0.6 + rng() * 1.4) * (i + 1) * 0.0018,
    p: rng() * Math.PI * 2,
    a: amp / (i + 1.2),
  }));
  const pts: string[] = [];
  for (let x = -40; x <= W + 40; x += 16) {
    const y = waves.reduce((s, w) => s + w.a * Math.sin(w.f * x + w.p), base);
    pts.push(`${x.toFixed(1)} ${y.toFixed(1)}`);
  }
  // Closed 700 units below the frame, not at its edge: the plane rises as she
  // scrolls, and the skirt is what keeps the ground beneath it painted.
  return `M -40 ${H + 700} L ${pts.join(" L ")} L ${W + 40} ${H + 700} Z`;
}
interface Blade {
  d: string;

3:description: Two registers under one roof - a dark marble hall at the door, a quiet neutral workroom inside.
5:  # ── The Hall (unauthenticated register; dark ground) ──
16:  # ── The Workroom (authenticated register; light is the shipped default) ──
42:  teacher-accent-hover: "#af7318"
100:    height: "44px"

109:    height: "44px"

126:    height: "44px"
141:One institution with two registers, and the seam between them is deliberate.
145:**The Workroom** is everything past sign-in. Light, neutral, and quiet, because a student sits inside it for an hour reading dense argument text and the interface must not compete with the passage. There is no warmth here and that is the decision, not an oversight: warm accents need a dark ground to read, and on the Workroom's white they collapse. The Hall's bronze measures 8.58:1 on its own night and 2.30:1 on the Workroom's page. The two registers do not share a palette because they cannot.
152:- Two registers, one institution: a dark marble Hall at the door, a light neutral Workroom inside.
165:- **Hall Bronze** (`#c6a467`): the Hall's warm light. Hairline borders on the call to action, a sheen across it on hover, and the selection highlight. It exists only on the Hall's night ground and never crosses into the Workroom.
170:- **Teacher Amber** (`#c7841f`): the single accent of the teacher workspace, with a hue-locked ramp for hover (`#af7318`), text on tinted fills (`#895910`) and a light step (`#e0a648`). Deliberately theme-independent: the workspace is always the light format. It is the one accent in the product, and it belongs to the operator, not the student.
181:- **The RRC door's cream is the owner's exception to his own ruling.** The Report Card brief vetoes cream ("overdone and too AI looking", 2026-09-08). The door (`.sl`, `--rd-cream`) was locked on cream three days earlier, on the same owner's brief, and stays on it: the door is a landing page in a painted evening and the card is a working document, and the two were never asked to share a ground. The contradiction is real and it is deliberate. An audit that flags it has found the record, not a defect.
193:- **Correct** (`#10b77f`) and **Incorrect** (`#f43e5c`): a student's attempt was right or wrong. Held under their own names, separate from system status, so a future shift to one cannot silently reframe the other.
255:The Hall is the exception and keeps its own depth: fixed ambience gradients, a grain overlay, and an inset highlight on its call to action. That depth is atmospheric rather than structural, and it does not transfer.
273:**The Hairline Rule.** One pixel, always. A thicker border used as a coloured accent stripe on the leading edge of a surface is banned, including when faked with an inset shadow. Active state is carried by background tone.
279:- **Character:** precise and quiet. A button is a plain rectangle with a label. It darkens slightly on hover and does nothing else. It does not lift, does not cast a shadow, and does not scale on press.
283:- **Ghost:** no fill at rest, a tonal fill on hover.
284:- **Sizes:** default 44px tall, small 40px, large 48px, icon 44 by 44. Every size clears the 44px touch minimum by being that height, never by relying on wrapped text or padding accidents.
286:- **Hall CTA:** 60px tall, near-square corners, transparent fill over a bronze hairline, with a bronze sheen that sweeps across on hover and a cyan outline on focus.
298:- **Style:** page background, one-pixel field border, 6px corners, 44px minimum height, 8px by 12px padding.
330:- **Don't** use a border thicker than one pixel as a coloured accent stripe, including when faked with an inset shadow.
332:- **Don't** animate layout properties. Animate `opacity`, `transform` and `color`. No bounce, no elastic, no lift on hover.
370:5. Components are tactile today, not quiet. Buttons lift, scale on press, and gain
371:   a shadow on hover; cards carry `shadow-sm`. The record above chooses quiet and flat.
....
68:- **Voice: undecided, deliberately.** The previous record carried a "coach voice, not industry voice" principle and two named voice registers (R1 procedural, R2 metaphor-led). Both were removed on 2026-09-05 by Joshua's decision, on the grounds that they were not stated clearly enough to be usable. They are being redone. Until a replacement is written, no work should cite the old voice rules, and no work should invent a voice rule to fill the gap. Route student-facing copy to Joshua.
76:- Real student work in the database: attempts, sessions, flags, wrong answers, assignments.
91:3. **Attempt before explanation.** The student commits to an answer before the product explains anything. Passive reading does not build the skill. Reveal copy addresses what the student actually chose, not a generic key.
99:**WCAG 2.1 AA is a binding floor across every surface.** It is a defect gate, not an aspiration. A finding below the AA bar is a bug and is fixed, not argued.
104:- Touch targets are 44x44px, and meet it by being that size rather than by a side effect such as wrapped text. This one is a house standard above the floor, not part of it: 44x44 is WCAG 2.5.5, which is Level AAA. The binding target-size rule is 2.5.8 at 24x24, so a control that clears 24x24 on a fine pointer clears the floor and is not a defect.
105:- A dense row of marks that exists to be read at a glance is the one place the 44x44 house standard yields. The Report Card's ledger is 22 to 26 ticks inside an 820px card; at 44px wide they would need 968 to 1144px, so meeting the standard means scrolling or wrapping, and the ledger stops being a single picture of the run. They are 44 tall and 28 to 30 wide, they clear 2.5.8, and that is the settled answer rather than an open defect. Joshua's call, 2026-09-09.
106:- Where a control has to stay small because it sits in chrome set in 11 or 13px type, the 44x44 target may be an explicit transparent overlay centred on the control instead of the control's own box. An overlay is a target somebody designed and it is proved by hit-testing the four edges; wrapped text is an accident and still does not count. The runner's rail controls, the door's crumb out and the demonstration's Play button are all built this way.

## assistant

Assessment A of the Read, Recall, Colour drill, from source on branch rrc-step-12 and the dev server at :8080. Viewport in the browser pane was 722x698, so the door rendered in its two-column beat layout and the card at 675px wide. Everything below is either measured in the DOM or cited to a line; where I could not verify, I say so.

One caveat that matters for every other assessment: `src/dev/sectionBeatPreview.tsx:18` imports only `section.css`, while `reportCardPreview.tsx:27` and `sectionLaunchPreview.tsx:124,176` import `index.css`. So the beat harness has no Tailwind `sr-only` rule, and the visually-hidden `<h1>` at `SectionStage.tsx:305` renders as a 321x59 block in the first grid cell of the rail. Measured: rail 162px tall, the progress rail collapsed to 0px wide, "Leave" wrapped to a second row at 321x23, and the paper's footer (instruction, hint, presence bar) sat at y 732 to 776 in a 698px viewport, below the fold. Injecting the one `.sr-only` rule brought the rail to 48px, the footer to 646 to 690, and the Leave overlay measured 44x59. The product does not ship this; the harness does. Any detector run against `section-beat-preview.html` is measuring a broken rail, and I did not count it below.

**1. AI-slop verdict**

Runner (`.ac-section`): clean. No tells found.

Report Card (`.rc`): mostly clean. No gradients, no glass, no lifts, no side stripes (explicitly removed, `SectionReportCard.tsx:552-556`). The residual tell is the console's Held / Understood / Time trio (`SectionReportCard.tsx:580-618`): label, big number, caption, three across, which is the hero-metric template even though the captions are real sentences.

Door (`.sl`): reads as designed by a very good LLM that has read the anti-slop list. Every tell is either removed with a comment saying so, or kept with a comment defending it. What is still there:
- Scroll-reveal fade-up on every block below the hero, `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:52-57`, applied at 335-341, 361-367, 528, 564-570. Decorative motion.
- Headline parallax with blur and scale on scroll, `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:183-189, 280`.
- Four identical illustrated step cards on gradient backdrops, `StepScene` at 68-123, gradients at `section-launch.css:864-875`, looping animations at 907, 963, 1012, 1045. The file's own comment at line 66 says the scenes carry "nothing the words do not."
- Three-card "Retelling / Reciting / Listing" grid with one card inverted to indigo and a 60px shadow, `section-launch.css:1242-1247`. The highlighted-pricing-tier pattern.
- Closing panel: four-stop indigo gradient with a radial apricot glow, `section-launch.css:1614-1624`, under a 58px italic display line.
- Typewriter, caret and waveform in the demo, `RecallDemo.tsx:81-110`. Functional here, but it is the "AI is typing" trope.
- A painted five-plane parallax meadow behind the hero, `MeadowScene.tsx`, `mw-sway` keyframes at `section-launch.css:172`.

Not found: gradient text, backdrop blur (only `backdrop-filter: none` at 1912), hover lifts (removed, comment at 317-319), accent stripes.

**2. Nielsen, whole journey (0 to 4)**

1. Visibility of status: 3. The rail says "Recording" (role=status, `SectionStage.tsx:337-341`) while the footer says "The section has not started." (578-581). True, and contradictory to read.
2. Match to the real world: 2. The console needs a mid-hero glossary to define "the move" (`SectionReportCard.tsx:566-570`); "Every cylinder on this one." (1178) is an idiom; the crumb "Out loud" (`SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:257, 271`) is a category that appears nowhere else.
3. User control and freedom: 3. Pause, Escape-means-stay, Check again, Leave with a real confirm (`SectionStage.tsx:252-282, 379-409`). No going back is by design.
4. Consistency and standards: 1. See priority 1. Four names for the same figure and two names for the same record.
5. Error prevention: 2. See priority 3. The unheard beat counts her into a run anyway.
6. Recognition over recall: 3. The method is on screen everywhere, arguably too everywhere (priority 4). The hint "space recall full stimulus" (`SectionStage.tsx:618-627`) is a fragment.
7. Flexibility and efficiency: 3. Roving tabindex and arrows on the ledger (724-750), Home/End on the beats (192-207). A returning student still gets a 4413px door.
8. Aesthetic and minimalist: 2. Door 4413px tall at 722 wide; console 848px tall on a 698 viewport before the first stimulus opens; the demo, four cards, a drawer and an aside all teach the same four steps.
9. Error recovery: 4. Refused mic names the consequence and the embedded-frame case (`SectionStage.tsx:465-510`); "That is ours, not yours." (`SectionReportCard.tsx:660-680`); the wait screen gives her the run id to quote (`SectionReport.tsx:214-219`); the dispute control never claims a save it did not make (1261-1300).
10. Help and documentation: 3. Plenty. The lede says "skim the rules above and go" (`instructions.json:27`) and `rules` never render on the briefing page, which its own comment admits (`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:397-399`).

Total: 26 of 40.

**3. Cognitive load: 3 failures of 8**

Decision points counted: door hero (3: pass), method section (4 cards + 2 pane tabs + Previous/Next = 7: fail), library row (2 to 3: pass), close panel (1: pass), briefing page (4 controls, but 8 text blocks before the foot Start: pass on options), refused beat (Ask again, Open in own tab, Leave this section, Leave = 4: pass, barely), running (Pause, Leave, Space, Hide this = 4: pass), Report Card console (22 ticks + 3 figures + 5 notes + glossary: fail) and the open stimulus (6 H3 movements, Hear it back, The passage, Everything else, Hear it x2, dispute, Previous, Next = 8 controls: fail).

**4. Working**

- The ready beat is honest about which of its states it is in and the refused state gives her two real ways forward, including the preview-frame case (`SectionStage.tsx:465-547`). This is the best screen in the journey.
- The Report Card tells the truth about its own failures before its number, in her terms (`SectionReportCard.tsx:660-680`, `SectionReport.tsx:167-173, 214-219`).
- The ledger as one picture of the run, one tab stop, something always open (`SectionReportCard.tsx:405-412, 724-770`).

**5. Priority issues**

1. The record has two names and the figure has four. `SectionLibrary.tsx:334` "Attempt 2", `:114` "91% carried"; `SectionLaunch.tsx:197` "Last run", `:258` "Your last 3 runs", `:288` column "Held"; `SectionReport.tsx:167` "Marking your run"; `SectionReportCard.tsx:526` "Attempt 2", `:581` "Held", `:1374` "Run these again"; `RecallDemo.tsx:291` "details carried"; the console glossary at 569 calls it "Coverage". She reads "carried" on the row, "Held" on the door's table, "run" on the strip, "Attempt" on the card. Fix: "Attempt" is the noun for the record, "run" is only a verb; "Held" is the figure everywhere. Change `SectionLibrary.tsx:114` to `${mean}% held`, `SectionLaunch.tsx:197,258` to "Last attempt" / "Your last 3 attempts", `SectionReport.tsx:167` to "Marking your attempt", and cut "Coverage" from 569.

2. The closing line is hardcoded and contradicts the aside. `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:587` "Twenty-five minutes. No going back." The goal is a prop (`SectionLaunch.tsx:24, 127` renders "est 25 min") and scales with an accommodation (`SectionRunner.tsx:1227-1228`; `instructions.json` section 6). "No going back" sits 600px under "You can pause" (`SectionLaunch.tsx:338-340`). Fix: pass `timeGoalMinutes` into the frame and render it, or drop the number; replace "No going back" with "One take."

3. The unheard beat starts the run it was built to prevent. `SectionStage.tsx:533-544`: "Nothing heard yet. Check it is not muted." rendered beside "Starting in 3", because `micChecked` goes true on `checkExpired` (`SectionRunner.tsx:242, 1237`). `VoiceLevel.tsx:689-697` explains the whole apparatus exists so a muted input does not produce "a Report Card of zeros" discovered at minute twenty-one. Fix: when the check expires unheard, hold, and offer "Start anyway" beside "Ask again"; count down only on `micHeard`.

4. The method is taught six times before stimulus one. Demo (`RecallDemo.tsx`), four cards (`SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:382-406`), drawer "This step" (452-477), "Full instructions" essay (479-508), the "Before you start" aside (`SectionLaunch.tsx:335-346` via `MethodSummary`), then the briefing page's four steps (`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:408-421`) plus its long version (427-466), then the coach for three stimuli (`coach.ts:78-111`). The door is 4413px. Fix: the door keeps the demo and the four cards; "Read the full instructions" goes to the briefing page (which exists for this) and the drawer goes; the aside keeps the two facts and loses `MethodSummary`.

5. "Choose a section" on a page with one section. `SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:471-474` and `515-518` render "Choose a section" as the drawer's last-step button on both doors; on the section page `goToSections` (226-234) scrolls her to her own run history. The sr-only H2 at 572-574 also names that slab "Choose a section" on the section page, and the sr-only H2 at 582-584 names the library's close panel "Start" when its button reads "Choose a section". Fix: thread a `slabLabel` prop (or derive from `sectionsFirst`): section page gets "Your attempts" and a "Start the section" button; library keeps "Choose a section".

6. The judge speaks in the third person on a second-person card. `SectionReportCard.tsx:1009-1012` renders `readReason` raw: "She said the consultant may be assuming..." under "Your Read", and it is not gated on `readMatches`, so it can sit under "You found the move." (verified in the clean preview at stimulus 1; that is harness data, but the code allows it). Fix: render the reason only when `!s.readMatches`, and either rewrite the judge's output to second person or prefix it "The marker's note:".

**6. Minor**

- Hint fragments in the beat: "space in a moment", "space once the microphone is on" (`SectionStage.tsx:618-627`).
- "Last run / most recent" restates itself (`SectionLaunch.tsx:197-201`).
- "Worth working on" ends with praise (`SectionReportCard.tsx:476-479`).
- "Hear it done right" (839) on a 100% stimulus implies hers was done wrong.
- The console gives the same verdict three ways in its first 80px: band, "15 of 22 at the standard", "Held 82%" (526-535, 581).
- The door strip has no Held figure at all (`SectionLaunch.tsx:196-242`: Last run, Trend, Recorded, Target) while Held is the card's headline; the table under it does carry Held (288).
- The library hero's only action is "scroll down" (`SectionLaunchFrame.tsx:307-311`) and the hero is 1037px on a 698px viewport, so the page's real content starts one and a half screens down.
- Measured contrast all clears AA: rail hint #64748b on white 4.76:1 at 13px, console 10px labels roughly 6:1, demo footer 10px roughly 5.5:1. No em dashes in any rendered student copy (checked all nine files and the instructions JSON; the dashes live in comments).
- "Stimulus 1 of 26" in the beat harness against "25 stimuli" on the door is harness data.

**7. Two questions**

1. The demo performs the method and the briefing page exists specifically to explain it before anything records. Who is the four-card grid, the drawer and the painted meadow for, given that every student who sees this door already has the drill assigned? If the door were the briefing page with the demo on top, what would she lose?

2. The card leads with a band because "a band, not a percentage" was the ruling (`SectionReportCard.tsx:32`), and then gives the percent, the count, three figures and a glossary in the same console. Which one number does she carry to the door tomorrow, and why does the door's strip not show it?
